# HANDOFF - APK ReLab

Read BLUEPRINT.md first (architecture, decisions, roadmap). This file tracks the current build.

## Workflow
Claude builds the project -> sends a zip -> owner opens it in Code On the Go (CoGo) and builds -> owner reports errors/screenshots -> Claude sends the next versioned zip.
Owner speaks Egyptian Arabic, wants concise, actionable answers.

## Known limits (accepted by the owner: notes, NOT bugs - do not "fix" unless asked)
- DEX files with version >= 040 (and the newer container formats) are untested; Opcodes.getDefault() / the dex-version -> API mapping in Rebuild.kt only covers 035/037/038/039.
- (Edge-to-edge insets: handled since 0.24.0 as part of the redesign - see below; still unverified on device.)
- (AI cache eviction: LRU since 0.69.2, a cache hit refreshes the file time; MAX_FILES = 300.)

  - Hybrid: the inspection is a product of the RESOURCE scan (APK load); `HybridInspector.clear()` at the start of a code scan wiped it whenever the resource scan finished first (Axis Pay 3.1.0 report 9). Now `clearApk()` at load, plus a source guard test. NOT a branch problem: main has had HybridInspector since PR #2.

Now FindingPolish.lowerUnforwardedPrivateIntentFlows covers every engine's cards: a not-exported Activity/Service's Intent flow drops one level and reach is YES~ unless TaintIr recorded a strong ICC write into it (TaintIr.forwardedComponents, written at WRITE time so analysis groups meet) or an open redirector exists.

  - Pillar 3a housekeeping (owner, 3b session): (1) drift.simonbinder.eu on Zulip is recorded as KNOWN NOISE (1 app) and is NOT added to NativeScanner.noiseHosts:
    a general deny entry needs the pattern in >= 3 apps (rule 4). (2) Reading ONE libapp.so (arm64-v8a first) is a permanent invariant: the same snapshot is
    compiled per ABI, so reading every copy triples the string scan on a multi-ABI APK for the same strings.
  - Pillar 3b (React Native, Cordova / Capacitor, audit_brief, NOT SEEN): HybridInspector now returns one Section per framework; HybridResult keeps the 3a
    properties (kind = the first detected, the 3a fields = the first section's) and adds kinds (FLUTTER, REACT_NATIVE, CORDOVA order, empty for a native app).
    Detection: REACT_NATIVE = (libreactnativejni.so or libhermes.so) and assets/index.android.bundle; CORDOVA = (assets/www/ or assets/public/) and (config.xml or
    assets/capacitor.config.json). RN: a Hermes bundle's string table through HermesStrings unchanged (the prefix up to the end of the string storage, under the
    asset scan's 48 MB cap; an unsupported version is named, not guessed); otherwise the bundle is read as text under the 10 MB cap. A long minified line is read
    in 1,800-char windows overlapping by 400 (SecretFormats.scan reads at most 2,000 chars); a value cut by a window's end is skipped there because the next
    window holds it whole (the first test of the boundary found the cut prefix counted as a second secret: fixed, with the test). No JS syntax is parsed.
    Cordova: res/xml/config.xml is binary XML in an APK (decoded with Axml; a text copy read as it is), only a <widget> document counts; every
    <access origin> / <allow-navigation href> value is listed, "*" is noted and left to the asset scan's card; capacitor.config.json's server.hostname /
    androidScheme / cleartext / allowNavigation listed verbatim (metadata, not findings); the text files of assets/www and assets/public read for endpoints and
    credential formats. audit_brief: one line per framework ("Flutter: libapp.so, 5 endpoints, 1 secret, 1 config file", "React Native: index.android.bundle, 2
    endpoints, 1 secret", "Cordova: assets/www, 2 named origins, 1 secret"; none for a native app); the UI line is the same lines joined.
    Verdict NOT SEEN: built from Coverage.hybridKinds ("Dart code (Flutter libapp.so): strings read, code not analysed", "JavaScript (React Native bundle): ...",
    "JavaScript (Cordova / Capacitor www): ..."; one per kind; none for a native app). The 1.2.0 wording was NOT a static "Dart not read": it came from the
    native-flutter and assets-coverage cards; those card-based lines now stay ONLY as a fallback when HybridInspector detected nothing but a card says a
    hybrid layer was seen (e.g. a React Native bundle whose libhermes.so is in a split that was not imported), so NOT SEEN never loses a line it had. No
    existing Verdict test changed (Verdict120Test's hybrid test passes cards and no kinds: the fallback). The other four Verdict lines are untouched.

  - Pillar 1.1 (async dispatch, measured first): before, on Axis Pay 3.0.1 with MeasureBig (the device's grouped pipeline, -Xmx2g, 2 threads), real-source drops at
    java/util/concurrent/Executor 97, ExecutorService 72, android/os/Handler 44, 15 flows; and the engine lost a value captured by a Runnable's constructor even on a
    direct run() call (test directRunCallCarriesTheCapturedField failed before). Built: (a) CTOR_CAPTURE: the summary of a constructor of a Runnable / Callable /
    kotlin FunctionN class records "parameter k stored into field F of this"; the caller writes its REAL taint into F's heap slot (fields typed java/lang/Object are
    skipped: measured on K-9, capturing them added 3 LOW flows through R8-merged / erased fields, and capturing in every class added 14 LOW flows on Axis Pay);
    (b) CallGraph "async" edges from Executor.execute / ExecutorService.submit / Handler.post / postDelayed / postAtFrontOfQueue / runOnUiThread to run() / call() of
    the candidate class of the Runnable argument (new-instance, check-cast or field type within 12 instructions, through moves), which also makes run() REACHABLE
    from the dispatching entry; TaintIr follows the edge only when the SSA value resolves to ONE concrete class (new-instance, a cast to or a field of a leaf
    class, moves, a phi of one class) and then counts no drop; (c) LAMBDA_DEVIRT: FunctionN.invoke on a receiver of one known class goes to that class only.
    After (same tool, same APKs; ./private/2.0.0_pillar1_corpus.txt): Axis Pay Executor + ExecutorService + ThreadPoolExecutor real drops 169 -> 62 (Executor 97 -> 0), all real
    library drops 4,340 -> 4,244, flows 15 -> 15; K-9 0 -> 0, NewPipe 1 -> 1, KeePassDX 9 -> 9, Paperless 1 -> 1, Chaka 1 -> 1 flows. So: drops recovered, no new card on
    this corpus (no new TP either: none of these apps has a captured-source flow that reaches a sink). The 62 left are ExecutorService.submit sites whose Runnable
    class is not resolved (a call result or another group's class).

- Maturity protocol (after 1.2.1; one PR per phase, each stacked on the previous one while it waits for the owner's merge):
  - Phase 0 (PR #2): company APK deleted from main's tree (history kept, owner's choice); binary guards (.gitignore, .pre-commit-config.yaml, .githooks/pre-commit); HANDOFF.md and PERF.md moved to docs/internal/; README, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, CHANGELOG, CONTRIBUTING, LICENSE (proprietary).

Verified locally: hygiene OK on 477 files and fails on a .so and on an 1,100,000-byte file (a 1,048,576-byte file passes); ir 330/330; Gradle 751 tests / 0 failures, lint OK. NOT verified locally: the workflow itself (actionlint was not run: its download was refused by the session's safety check); the first CI run is its test. typecheck_app.sh is not in CI (it needs the local ~/tc toolchain).
  - Phase 2: the MUST FIX gate (`MustFixGate.kt`): a HIGH+ card enters MUST FIX only when (a) its sink is high-impact (TaintIr's HIGH / CRITICAL sinks: exec, code loading, WebView, SQL, file open / write / delete, deserialization, reflection by name, Intent launch; not SSRF, getMethod, logging), (b) its exported entry has no SIGNATURE-level guard (signature / signatureOrSystem / privileged / system, or a platform BIND_* permission; dangerous and normal permissions such as READ_SMS are NOT guards), (c) no validation-like call was seen on the path, (d) it has an SSA flow or an exact reach path from its manifest entry (a YES~ class-level path of a rule card is not a proof). No CWE weights; the score only orders the cards that pass. PermissionGuard.strengthOf now falls back to ReportBuilder.defaultLevel (BIND_* and the known platform signature permissions are SIGNATURE without a device resolver). Verdict.build takes the guard (MainActivity: PermissionGuard with the system resolver; SandboxScan: no resolver). MustFixGateTest 9 tests (READ_SMS stays unprotected and enters MUST FIX; signature, signatureOrSystem, privileged|system and BIND_JOB_SERVICE keep it out); 4 switch mutants + 6 source mutants killed. Three 1.2.1 tests changed on purpose (a validated card is no longer a MUST FIX; a rule card needs a high-impact sink and an exact path; BIND_* unresolved is SIGNATURE). Gradle 760 tests / 0 failures, lint 0 errors, assembleDebug OK (MainActivity compiled; not run on a device). Hash lock: self_check_apps.json carries package / versionName / sha256 and self_check.sh refuses a different file (tested). top3_accuracy added to self_check.py (9 Python tests, 4 mutants killed). Result: recall unchanged on all six; top3 13/16 = 81% with the gate and the same counts without it (sandbox: only SSA cards have reach). qa_120.md is labelled TUNING SET. Details: qa_120.md "phase 2".
  - Phase 3 (context model): (a) ProviderCodePermissionRule: a data method that only throws counts as closed (THROWING_IS_CLOSED), checkSignatures is a caller check; K-9 #7 HIGH -> LOW. (b) FindingPolish.lowerGuardedProviderFlows (PROVIDER_CONTEXT): a ContentProvider-parameter taint card in a provider that is not exported or is opened only by a signature-level permission drops one level; Nextcloud #58 HIGH -> MEDIUM; #44 stays HIGH (writers are unresolved method parameters: not provable by the linear rule). (c) TaintIr path-sanitizer summary (PATH_SANITIZER_SUMMARY, -Dapkrelab.noPathSanitizerSummary): a callee whose canonical-path check (framework startsWith / equals by name, a renamed static (File,File)Z / (String,String)Z / (Path,Path)Z helper by shape, on the canonical value) is tested by if-eqz / if-nez and whose every reference return lies on the passing side returns clean; a check that some return skips keeps the taint as a weak (LOW) flow with a "pathcheck-partial" sentence. Paperless #32 gone (F-Droid APK re-downloaded, hash = index). Tests: Phase3ContextTest 9, IrPathSanitizerSummaryTest 17; mutants: 3a 3/3, 3b 3/3, 3c 20/20 killed + 3 switches. - 1.2.0 "The Verdict". Version 1.2.0 / code 224. Gradle 736 tests / 0 failures, lint 0 errors (198 warnings), assembleDebug OK (MainActivity changes compiled by the real build; not run on a device); Kotlin 406 and ir 330 (sandbox runners); self_check 5 Python tests. Why this release exists: 1.1.0 finds issues (706 tests, lint 0, self-scan HIGH 0, 24 / 24 MASVS, grouped analysis under 512 MB), but a reader of its report still has to sort the cards, decide which are real, cross-reference the attack scenarios, choose what to fix first and remember what was NOT scanned. 1.2.0 closes that gap with data the scanner already produces: a verdict at the top of every report, a visible per-card confidence, correlated findings, one-sentence fixes, and a corpus self-check before every release.
  - **What 1.2.0 does NOT do**: no new detection (every finding of 1.2.0 existed in 1.1.0); no execution of the app (the tool only reads); no patch generation (fix suggestions are sentences); no business-logic analysis; no native disassembly; no Frida, no adb commands, no reproduction steps; no strings-overlay work beyond 0.102.0. It changes no severity, no finding and no flow: only how they are read.
  - Item 1 Verdict (`Verdict.kt`): VERDICT / CRITICAL PATH / MUST FIX (max 3: HIGH+ and reach YES / YES~, by FindingScore, with the item-4 sentence) / CONFIDENCE (share of HIGH+ cards with confidence HIGH: strong >= 80%, moderate 50-80%, weak) / NOT SEEN (native methods + libraries read, reflection not followed + reflection-sink cards, decrypted / total decryptor call sites, splits, capped groups / time limit), at the top of the MD and HTML reports and in SARIF run.properties.verdict; SCAN FAILED when the deep scan read 0 classes. Reflection: the engine counts no reflection sites, so the line says "not followed" and the number of reflection-sink cards (no new plumbing). One accessor added: PostureScanner.decryptorCallCount (a count it already kept).
  - Item 2 Confidence (`FindingScore.confidence`): 0-100 = severity base x reach x source (Intent / JS bridge 1.0, provider 0.9, prefs 0.7, library 0.5, none 1.0) x evidence (SSA flow 1.0, class-level flow 0.8, rule text 0.6, validation-like calls seen on the path 0.3: the sentence SsaBridge / the class-level taint rule write from their validation pattern), factors and a "Why" line under every card (MD, HTML, SARIF properties).
  - Item 3 Correlation (`Correlation.kt`): MEDIUM+ cards linked by a shared entry method (reach chain), tainted field ("through field X") or class; groups of two or more get "Related findings: #a, #b. Suggested fix: ..." (sink, entry, field or class). The sink-in-class edge of the brief is implied by the class edge (a mutant of it could never fail) and was dropped as a link; it still names the group's fix.
  - Item 4 Fix templates (`FixTemplates.kt`): WebView URL, file WRITE (sink-checked: a FileInputStream card gets none), SQL args, TrustManager, JS bridge, exported component (names the app package, points to queue_manifest_fix). "Suggested fix:" under the card's own Fix; unknown rules keep only their own text.
  - Item 5 Self-check (`tools/sandbox/self_check.sh` + `self_check.py`, `tools/benchmark/self_check_apps.json`, `bench.py --evidence`): recall per app with the matching card, judged-TP regressions against `tools/benchmark/baselines/<release>/`, flow set new / lost; exit 1 on a lost TP or a missing report. RELEASE PROCESS from 1.2.0: run it before every release and put its "Recall:" line in the release notes; any change to the engine, a rule or the call graph carries those numbers; write the baseline (--write-baseline) at each tagged release after the numbers were reviewed.
  - Gates: 30 Kotlin tests (Verdict 8, Confidence 8, Correlation 6, FixTemplates 8) with twins; 10 mutants, all killed after one twin was added (an unreachable CRITICAL card must not be a must-fix: the first cap test hid it behind the top-3 cut). Before / after with the 1.1.0 and 1.2.0 scanners on the same decode (SandboxScan): Flowit (clean) 4 cards and webviewkiosk 49 cards / 15 SSA items, card lists identical; the verdicts are quoted in PERF.md "1.2.0".

  - **Hermes bytecode strings** (`HermesStrings.kt`, used by `AssetScanner`): the layout was taken from Hermes' own `BytecodeFileFormat.h` / `BytecodeStream.cpp` (fetched from GitHub, version 96: 128-byte header, function headers 16 B, string kinds, identifier hashes, small string table 4 B = isUTF16:1 / offset:23 / length:8 with 255 = overflow, overflow table 8 B, storage; each section aligned to 4). Only version 96 is read (other versions move header fields; the card says so), a file that is not self-consistent is refused, nothing throws (3,000 corrupted copies in a test). Validated on the 3 real React Native bundles of the sandbox: all parsed (7,406 / 23,517 / 23,964 strings, 5-8 ms each, over 99.9% printable) and the whole asset scan of the 34 APKs is unchanged except the Hermes cards now read "string table scanned, code not". **0 raw secret-format hits, 0 cards on about 55,000 real strings: recall on a real bundle is UNMEASURED** (no positive exists in the set; a synthetic spec-valid file is what proves the path). 4 tests + 7 mutants killed.

Only a group that finished normally is stored (never cancelled / out-of-memory / failed ones; a group with an unreadable class is never cached); any damaged file is a miss. The merge, the status statistics and the picked order are unchanged. **Real data (Paperless, 8,389 classes treated as app code, 19 groups, 512 MB heap, 1 thread; flows checksum identical in every run)**: no cache 9.7 s wall; cold with cache 7.5 s (it also writes 19 files); **unchanged rescan 1.5 s, 19 of 19 groups reused**; after commenting 3 classes: **11 of 19 reused**, 5.1 s against 7.9 s without a cache; restored: 19 of 19 again. 6 tests (warm == uncached, one-class edit, engine / entries / earlier findings invalidate, unreadable class, truncated file, field-by-field disk round trip, trim) and 8 mutants killed. **Limits to remember**: (1) the planner pass (reading every class, 16-29 s on Axis Pay) and the hash pass (one read of each unique class) still run on every scan: only the analysis, which was 544-1,016 s there, is saved; (2) groups overlap (each class is in about 10 slots, Axis: 25 groups), so editing one class re-runs every group it is in, not one: 3 classes touched 8 of 19 groups here; (3) results are only valid for the same engine id: do not edit engine code without bumping versionCode; (4) NOT run on a device: cold-scan overhead there (writing the files, the extra hash read) is unmeasured; the status line prints "N of M groups reused from the cache" and the GC time.

- 0.103.2 (the owner's review of the two device reports; Kotlin 311/311, `ir` 292/292). Version 0.103.2 / code 213. Each of the four points was checked against the code before acting; two premises did not hold.
  - **1. SCA inventory.** The 84-row report never reached the repository (only reports 2 and 3 were sent), and the stated cause (no splits) cannot change the package-path layer, which reads DEX types only. The question behind it was still worth checking: the Deep / Smart scans NEVER write the inventory; the only writer is `scanResourcesAsync`, called once per APK load. The real hazard is loading a SECOND apk while the first scan thread runs: that thread read `apkFile` when it ran (it could scan the new file) and rebuilt the shared inventory step by step (reset, exact libraries, probes, path rows), so a second apk or a report exported half-way saw a half-built or mixed list; only the UI publication had a "still the same apk" guard. Fix: the thread captures its apk, builds the inventory LOCALLY (`LibraryScanner.scanApkLocal`, pure `merge`) and publishes it once, under the lock, only if its apk is still the loaded one; the inventory is emptied when a new apk is loaded. `scanApk` / `mergeInventory` stay for old callers and tests. 3 tests, 2 mutants killed.
  - **2. Threads (the "2x" prediction did NOT hold).** Sandbox with 4 REAL cores this time (`MeasureBig -Dall=true -Dmax=1000` = Paperless, 8,389 classes treated as app code, 19 groups, flows identical in all 36 runs, no OOM; GC pauses summed from `-Xlog:gc`): heap 512 MB: 1 thread 7.3 s, **2 threads 6.4 s (-12%)**, 3 threads 6.1 s, 4 threads 6.4 s; heap 256 MB: 8.1 / 7.2 / 7.3 / 8.2 s; heap 160 MB: 9.2 / 9.0 / 10.4 / **17.7 s**. GC was about 35% of the wall time at 512 MB with ONE thread and grows with every thread (2.5 s -> 2.7 -> 3.2 s). The phone's own earlier report said "re-run for memory" with 2 threads. NOT measured: ART's collector (not G1), the phone's big.LITTLE and throttling, a 18k-class app. So the automatic rule (`heap / 1000`) is UNCHANGED; instead a new setting **"Analysis threads: auto / 1 / 2 / 3"** (next to the SSA time limit, capped by cores - 1) lets the owner A/B it on the phone with the GC numbers of the 0.103.1 status line. `MeasureBig` got `-Dall=true` for this kind of workload.
  - **3. Wrong risk text (verified, wider than reported).** The `CWE-693` knowledge entry held the BIOMETRIC-prompt text (the biometric rule uses CWE-287), so every CWE-693 card (native hardening, root / emulator / Frida detection) showed it. Fixed at the root: CWE-693 is now a generic "protection missing or only checked on the device" entry (M7), and `native-hardening` has its own entry (RELRO / NX / canary / PIE, the linker flags that fix it, M7 Insufficient Binary Protections). 1 test (positive + twins for the other CWE-693 rules), 2 mutants killed.
  - **4. `aq8` and the "runs" numbers.** The report only says `aq8` is a renamed class (scope OBF) with a `registerReceiver` card at about line 14,349 of its smali; WHICH SDK it belongs to cannot be read from the report (no APK / smali here): ask the agent `get_class aq8` or `callers` after a Deep scan. The claim that it is "analysed 73 times across the groups" is a statistics artifact: `TaintIr.Stats.merge` kept only the top 5 methods after every merge, so a method that left and re-entered the list lost its earlier counts (38 runs in one report, 73 in the next, same app). Fixed: 40 kept internally, 5 printed (a test; mutant killed). The slowest five methods add up to about 15 s of 497 s of analysis CPU (3%): a single big class is NOT the cost, the time is spread over 25 groups.

For comparison 0.99.0: 1,185 s on 2 threads (a different heap). NOT known: the GC share on the phone. **0.103.1 prints it**: the status line now ends with "GC x ms in n collections, y ms blocking" for the group loop (`Debug.getRuntimeStat("art.gc.*")`, wrapped in try/catch, returns 0 where a device lacks it). Cause: `StringOverlay.argRe` held a bare `}`; Java's regex (used by every unit test here) accepts it, Android's ICU engine throws `PatternSyntaxException` while the class initialises. Fix: braces escaped; `analyzeFile` and `agentIndexedText` now survive any overlay failure (the overlay is optional, the scan must not die with it). LESSON: the unit tests run on the JDK regex engine, so a regex that passes here can still fail on a device; keep every `{` and `}` escaped in new patterns and say "regex dialect not tested on ICU" when adding one. - 0.102.0 (installed-apps picker, SCA race fix, decoded-strings overlay, two deterministic guards; Kotlin 293/293 (277 + 16 new), `ir` 287/287). Version 0.102.0 / code 209. First session in the cloud container with the project re-imported from the owner's zip (`git log`: "Import APKReLab 0.101.1 sources"); the placeholder repo was emptied as asked. **The owner's two device reports (91 vs 84 SCA rows) and the APKs were NOT in the zip** (only the two 0.85.0 sandbox reports, which have no SCA section), so B could not be verified against data and nothing was scanned on a company app.
  - **New verification tool**: `tools/sandbox/typecheck_app.sh` type-checks the WHOLE app, `MainActivity.kt` / `Ui.kt` included, with kotlinc against `android-all` 16 and the real library jars. Result: only the generated `R` class is unresolved (6 messages); a planted bad identifier is caught. So the Android-only edits below are TYPE-CHECKED, not parse-checked; they are still not a Gradle build and not run on a device.
  - **A. Installed apps** (`MainActivity`: chip "Installed" -> filterable list -> `analyzeInstalledApp`; core `SplitApks.copyInstalled`, pure Kotlin): copies `ApplicationInfo.sourceDir` to `workspaces/p1/original.apk` and `splitSourceDirs` to `splits/`, the same layout the file picker makes, so `analyzeFile` / `NativeScanner.extraArchives` / "Last APK" need no change. Package visibility: `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` was already in the manifest; a device that still hides apps shows a short list. 3 tests (copy as original + splits and the native scanner reads the copied split; no splits / unreadable split; two splits with one name), mutant "do not copy" killed by 2. NOT run on a device: whether `base.apk` of every app is readable from our process (it is on stock Android for user apps, not verified here).
  - **B. SCA rows 91 vs 84: a real race found by reading, not yet shown to be THE cause.** `applyResult` sets `classDefs = emptyList()` and starts `scanResourcesAsync()` at once; that thread read `classDefs` after the resource + library passes. The class index is built lazily (`buildIndex`), so when it was not ready, `types` was empty and the package-path layer AND the Gson / Kotlin version probes were silently skipped (no retry): the table depended on timing. Fix: wait for `buildIndex()` (idempotent, locked) and, if it still fails, put "package-path SKIPPED (class index not available)" in the SCA stats. The other source of run-to-run difference in the same code is the wall-clock budget `MAX_MILLIS = 25 s` in `LibraryScanner.detect` (native `.so` strings and dex strings stop when it runs out: slower phone, fewer rows) - NOT changed. **To settle it, the owner greps the two reports for the `SCA: libraries N (...)` line: if one has `; package-path M` and the other does not, it is this race.**
  - **C. Decoded-strings overlay** (`StringOverlay.kt`, tests `StringOverlayTest`): `const vN, <int>` + `invoke-static {vN}, <decryptor>(I)Ljava/lang/String;` + `move-result-object vM` -> `const-string vM, "plaintext"` in the class text the scans read (`agentIndexedText`; the cached index, the smali viewer and the editor keep the raw text; the three offset-using sites in `MainActivity` now read the same text). Dump formats: lines `index<TAB>"text"` (JSON-quoted or raw, multi-line continuation) with a `#decryptor<TAB>Lvs/C;->d(I)Ljava/lang/String;` header, or JSON. The report's Frida snippet now prints that header and `JSON.stringify`. UI: "Strings" button next to the baseline buttons on the Security screen; the dump is kept as `workspaces/p1/strings.overlay` and reloaded with "Last APK". Without a header the app's dominant decryptor (`PostureScanner.dominantDecryptor`, needs a deep scan first) is used. Only a constant index in the same straight-line block (no label, no write in between) is resolved; everything else stays encrypted and is counted (the Defense posture section prints replaced / still-encrypted). 8 tests (positive; negative twins: index missing, parameter index, label, clobbered register, result not taken, another class's method with the same name; negative: overlay off), 4 mutants killed. Tested on SYNTHETIC smali only: no real dump, no real Flutter / protector app yet. Cost: a text that does not contain the decryptor reference is returned untouched (one `contains`); the grouped analysis re-reads classes about 10 times, each read of an affected class is rewritten again (not measured; the cache stays raw so memory does not grow).
  - **D. Deterministic guards** (`ConstantArgGuards.kt`, `ConstantArgGuardsTest`): (1) `crypto-cipher`: `AES/ECB/NoPadding` whose only uses in the method are `doFinal([B)` on one fresh 16-byte array that nothing else mentions (the AES-CMAC / CBC-MAC subkey step E_K(0^128)) -> LOW with the reasoning in the card; padding other than NoPadding, a 32-byte array, a written / passed-on array, a label, a parameter, a second `doFinal` on data, or `update()` keep HIGH. (2) `shell-exec`: when EVERY `Runtime.exec` / `ProcessBuilder(String...)` site in the class has only constant arguments (literal, a copy, a constant-indexed static `(I)String` lookup of a non-framework class, a String[] of those) -> LOW "with fixed arguments"; a parameter, a field, a framework `(I)String`, one non-constant element, or one non-constant site among constant ones keep MEDIUM. New posture signal `shell-const` ("Shell commands with fixed arguments"). 5 tests, 6 mutants killed. **Regression check without the apps**: the 57 injected smali classes of the recall kit through all legacy rules before and after: 74 findings, IDENTICAL (cases 22B / 27 / 45 stay `shell-exec` MEDIUM, 15 stays ECB HIGH). **Not measured: the before / after card count on the two company reports** (not available); the owner should look for the cards "Shell command execution" and "Weak cipher mode: ECB" on Axis Pay and WE Wallet after rescanning, and read each lowered card once. A decryptor-returned command is judged "constant" because its INDEX is constant, not because its plaintext is known: it could be any command; with the overlay loaded the command is a literal and visible.
  - **E (speed) and F (splits, `libapp.so`)**: not started: both need the owner's phone / APKs (E: two Deep-scan runs of Axis Pay with the status line; F: the splits through the new picker). Nothing was measured in this session, so PERF.md is unchanged.

The phone's own status line (WE Wallet, 2 threads) said "2 group(s) re-run for memory": it is memory-bound. Phone timing varies by about 36% run to run (same code, same app: groups 1,185 s then 1,613 s), so one device run cannot show a 10 to 20% gain.
  - **Done, result identical** (flows, plan, taint-loss counters and drop owners compared on Axis Pay, the wallet app, Fossify, OVAA, Jellyfin; `ir` tests incl. 2 new for the set): `CallGraph` keeps its edge dedupe as ints in a new `LongSet` (an open-addressing long array; the first version, a HashSet per pair, gave NO gain at 1 GB and was dropped) and memoises `dispatch`. Sandbox wall time at 3 GB: Axis 116 to 76 s, wallet 46 to 33, Fossify 31 to 22, Jellyfin 52 to 36; at 1 GB: 138 to 117 s (2 threads), 102 to 78 s (1 thread). `SsaGroups` thread count now follows the heap (`heapMb / 1000` instead of `/ 256`), result independent of it: at 1 GB one thread took 78 s with 23 s of GC against 117 s and 59 s for two. CAVEAT: the sandbox has ONE core, so it cannot show the gain of two threads on a multi-core phone: the policy only stops two groups from sharing a small heap. The status line now prints the heap and the cores.
  - **Not adopted**: a bigger group cap (5000 or 8000 classes): analysis CPU 137 s to 96 s and 55 s at 3 GB but NO gain at 1 GB, 8000 did not finish at 1 GB, and the flow SET differs by one flow (count equal): a different result. The other session's note in `tools/perf/README.md` said "same flows": corrected there.

The owner's device report of Axis Pay (built from 0.99.0) arrived with a five-phase plan from the other reviewer; what was verified and what was done is below.
  - **Device report of Axis Pay, verified**: 59 cards (1 HIGH = the BouncyCastle SCA card, 13 MEDIUM, 45 LOW), the string-decryption signal fired (40,049 call sites: same number as the sandbox), the shell-command card got an AI verdict FALSE POSITIVE 90%, no "Splits" line and the card "Native code not in this APK": the owner picked the base only (the splits are still missing). Deep scan: 30 groups, 1,185 s on 2 threads (about 20 minutes on the phone), 6,424 real taint drops of 189,023 (top owners are renamed classes). The reviewer's claim that a commercial obfuscator removed the Gson version constant is wrong for this app (R8: the constant class was shrunk).
  - **Premise of the plan's phase 1 was WRONG, measured on the wallet APK**: the classes named like Jackson methods (`_findCustomCollectionDeserializer`, `_findParamName` ...) are NOT Jackson: they are Guava (strings "Occurrences must not be negative.", "(this Collection)") and a launcher-badge library; the obfuscator draws class names from a dictionary of method names. Jackson itself is present under its own package with its own names (so a Jackson content fingerprint would change nothing there). Content markers for Guava / Gson / Protobuf / Tink found hits in classes with app-like names too (a marker string appears in app classes that merely mention a library): a marker table needs reading each hit; NOT built. This corrects what I wrote earlier in this project (I had accepted "Jackson classes renamed by the obfuscator" from the owner's note).
  - **Rules added** (`ScanRulesDb.kt`): `jackson-default-typing` (MEDIUM: `enableDefaultTyping()`, `activateDefaultTyping` with LaissezFaireSubTypeValidator, `@JsonTypeInfo(use = CLASS | MINIMAL_CLASS)`, in non-Jackson classes only: in the wallet app the only `enableDefaultTyping` hits are Jackson's own definitions) and `crypto-db-passphrase` (HIGH when the passphrase handed to `getWritableDatabase(String)` / `getReadableDatabase(String)` / SQLCipher `openOrCreateDatabase` / `SupportFactory(byte[])` is a constant, MEDIUM when derived from ANDROID_ID, serial, IMEI, package name; found by following the register in the method; any other source, a Keystore, a reflective call, a parameter, is NOT reported; the value is never printed). The `(String)` overload identifies SQLCipher whatever the receiver class is called (the wallet app's helper class is renamed). Mapped to MASVS (CODE-4; CRYPTO-2 + STORAGE-1).
  - **Measured**: recall kit 4 new injected cases (60 - 63): before 39 of 43 must-cases (the 4 new missed), after 43 of 43 at the expected severity; probes unchanged (9 of 10). Eight real apps rescanned (both company apps, Fossify Gallery, Seal, Session, Jellyfin, OVAA, NetGuard): 0 hits of the new rules and 0 changed cards on the three compared with their previous scans. Mutation check, 5 mutants (library not skipped, any validator accepted, unknown sources reported, SQLCipher library classes reported, the secret printed): all killed by 4 failing tests. Corpus note for the rules: across 28 decoded apps there is no SQLCipher passphrase call with a constant and no app-code default typing, so recall on real code is unmeasured (injected cases and unit tests only).
  - **Not built, on purpose**: the clipboard rule: `setPrimaryClip` is in 82 classes of 24 of 26 decoded apps (every app copies something), so a rule on the call alone is pure noise; a useful one needs to know the value is a secret (a password field, an OTP) and is a different, harder rule.
  - **Read by hand**: (1) Fossify Gallery's one HIGH (file path from `Intent.getData()` into `FileInputStream`, `MainActivity.fillExtraOutput`) is a false positive by design: the Intent is the RESULT returned by the app's own picker to `onActivityResult`, and the method copies the user's chosen media to the caller's EXTRA_OUTPUT uri. IDEA (not done): lower taint cards whose source is the `data` parameter of `onActivityResult` unless the started activity is implicit; count how many corpus cards that touches first. (2) The wallet app DOES use SQLCipher (through a patched ORMLite connection source calling the renamed helper's `getWritableDatabase(String)`), and its passphrase comes from a reflective call whose target could not be resolved statically: so whether the database key is constant is UNKNOWN; the owner was given a runtime check (hash the passphrase on two installs: the same hash on both means a constant or a derived-from-constants key).
  - **Concurrency hazard confirmed again**: the recall kit wrote to a fixed shared directory (`/home/claude/inj`); one of my runs returned results that differed wildly from the previous run (11 must-cases missed, many unrelated to the change) while the other session was evidently using the kit; `RECALL_WORK` now selects a private directory and a rerun with one gave the stable 39 / 43 and 43 / 43. Use it.
  - **Not done, plan items** (critique in the conversation): TaintIr Parcel / Method.invoke models (no evidence yet that the 6,424 drops hide a real flow: the top owners are renamed classes), the 20-minute scan time of a 18k-class app (the real lever is the group / hub planner, a separate phase), streaming for 80k+ class apps (a sandbox problem; the company apps scan on the phone), React Native / Cordova (neither company app uses them), IR tools for the agent, callee code in the AI triage prompt.

New rule: a helper that returns void and takes only primitives / primitive arrays plus a trailing `Object[]` out-array cannot receive the page's text, so ITS reflection hit is dropped (its other hits, files / processes ..., still count). 3 twin tests (plumbing dropped; a helper with a String parameter or an Object[] out-array with a String still counts; process execution through plumbing still counts). Rescans: the wallet's HIGH card is gone; Jellyfin's real bridge card (starting components) is kept; OVAA, Vuldroid and InsecureShop have no card of this rule before or after, so the corpus holds ONE real bridge card as evidence that true positives survive: thin, the twin tests carry the weight.

  - Nine sink kinds (shell, SQL, file path, loaded code, deserialization, intent redirection, class / method by name, chosen server, chosen file / content URI), each with impact, fix, MASVS and the Frida hooks of its API; the component is the one holding the SOURCE of the flow (`sourceMethod`), not the sink's class. Exported without a permission: HIGH, adb line from the extras the code reads (`IntentExtras`): by real names and types, every query parameter of a deep link, `startservice`, `broadcast -a <action>`, a benign probe for an open provider; extras that are Parcelable / Serializable / Bundle have no adb line (no invented `--es`). Not exported but on a call path from an exported component: MEDIUM with the writer of the extra named; dropped (and listed under "Not shown") when every writer puts a constant. A provider already covered by pattern 4 gets one scenario. The chain lists each finding once.
  - **Defects found by reading the first output, fixed before release**: a fake `--es <extra-name>` line followed by "(the extras X are not plain values)" for Parcelable-only extras; only the first query key on a deep link (InjuredAndroid's RCE reads three); an internal component's scenario without the names of the extras it reads; an exported provider treated as "reached through a path" instead of directly; the same finding three times in one chain.
  - **Measured by reading (sandbox, 17 apps finished at the time of writing; 12 more were still running)**: InjuredAndroid 2 exported-sink scenarios (RCE CRITICAL with `binary` / `combined` / `param`; intent redirection with a Parcelable extra), Vuldroid 2 (command injection through the extra `command`, MEDIUM because the activity is not exported; redirection), PIVAA 2 (the exported receiver with its real action and its extras `data` and `location`; SQL), Sieve 1 (DBContentProvider SQL probe), OVAA 1 (exported service, file path), InsecureShop 1 (redirection): all match the documented weaknesses of those apps. Clean apps: Markor 2 (an editor that opens the file it is sent: by design, but the flow rule says HIGH), 0 in K-9 Mail, Organic Maps, NetGuard, Session, DAVx5, Orbot, Read You, andOTP, Breezy and the owner's wallet app. Precision on clean apps: 2 scenarios in 12 apps, both Markor.

- 0.95.1 (previous):

No new `MainActivity` edit. **The owner uploaded the APK of his wallet app** (not stored in the repo; never sent to any API). It is the SAME build as his reports: 3,202 entries, classes.dex 12,421 KB / classes2 10,945 KB / classes3 351.4 KB, 55.1 MiB, bundletool-signed split (no lib/*.so). Full pipeline run in the sandbox: 67 cards (device: 69), 14 APP / 43 OBF / 10 OTHER, same scope picture as the device; the sandbox has no SCA cards and no AI triage.
  - **The scope bug's real cause, measured (my earlier hypothesis was WRONG)**: not brand / R classes under the manifest package (`com/TE` has 0 classes). The old `codePackage` picked `org.bouncycastle2`, a REPACKAGED BouncyCastle with 894 readable classes, over the app's own `com.emeint` (829 readable classes; 8,085 more renamed into the default package by the obfuscator). The components rule of 0.92.1 fixed it (132 manifest components, `com.emeint`); 0.95.1 also treats `org/bouncycastle*` as a library (even without the components rule the answer is now `com.emeint`), plus sleepbot / fourmob datetimepicker, akexorcist, lamudi phonefield. Cards unchanged by this; the "classes outside the known libraries" count dropped from 9,880 to 8,986 and the renamed share rose from 81% to 90%.
  - **New posture signal**: DexGuard-style string encryption (static `m([I[BZ[Object)V` decryptors: 65 classes) and reflection marshalling (a root-package static `(ICIIZString,Class[])Object` lookup used instead of direct calls: 1,133 classes). Fires on 0 of the 26 decoded corpus apps (precision only: no other DexGuard app to measure recall; the fingerprint is one DexGuard version's). Its text says what is blind in those classes (string constants, Intent extra names, call-graph edges).
  - **Read by hand in the smali (answers about his app, not tool output)**: the WebView sink in `SignUpWebViewActivity` loads a field set in `onCreate` from `getIntent().getStringExtra(<decrypted name>)`; the only launcher on the path (`LoginActivity.handleCreateNewAccountUsingWebView`) passes `getString(R.string.webview_signup_link)` (a string RESOURCE, name read from a reflective lookup) through a helper and `startActivityForResult`: the URL is fixed at build time, the activity is not exported. The value of that resource could NOT be read (resource names are stripped from the table): if it is `http://`, or the page's host is compromised, the bridged object is reachable; if it is `https://` on the company's own host, the chain needs a TLS interception or a compromised page. `IntentExtras` could not follow this chain (encrypted extra name, helper, reflection): the scenario kept its old text. The WebViewClient, WebChromeClient and the bridge object are created by reflection (not by direct `new`).
  - **Pinning, read by hand**: no `CertificatePinner` USAGE seen: the class exists (OkHttp 4, renamed to a root-package name) as library code; `sha256/` appears only inside it; no TrustKit, no X509TrustManager implementer, no `checkServerTrusted`; 2 HostnameVerifier implementers and 2 `SSLContext.init` users were not read. No network security config declared (targetSdk 37: platform defaults, system CAs only). So "no pinning detected" is plausible for this app but not proven under obfuscation: the posture row says so.
  - **Posture NSC row** now has three states (present / no config declared / not checked). Not done and unchanged: the exported-component-to-sink scenarios (InjuredAndroid's CRITICAL shell flow has none), split APKs, hybrid apps, streaming for huge apps, Parcel / Method.invoke taint, callee code in the AI triage prompt.

  - **First-run (blind) counts of set 11, 7 apps that finished**: InjuredAndroid 4 HIGH + 1 CRITICAL (all real: RCE, WebView XSS x2, intent redirection) and it MISSED the DES key challenge; K-9 Mail 1 HIGH (false: exported provider that checks the caller's package and signing certificate in code); Organic Maps 2 HIGH (false: static native getters without parameters in Parcelable data classes); Fossify Gallery 1 HIGH (file path from Intent.getData: NOT YET READ); Element X 0; Bitwarden Authenticator 0; Zulip (Flutter) 0 and that is BLINDNESS, not cleanliness. Failed to finish: DuckDuckGo (83k classes, out of memory twice in the 4 GB sandbox), ProtonVPN (out of memory / stuck), StreetComplete (my own mistake: I rebuilt the classes during its scan). So: 3 of the 4 HIGH cards I read on the clean apps were false; on the vulnerable app every HIGH was real.
  - **Fixes**: (1) hard-coded key rule now covers DESKeySpec / DESedeKeySpec and a key that reaches the spec from ANOTHER class (static byte[] filled in a `<clinit>` from a literal or Base64.decode, ()[B getter, copy into another class's field): resolved after the whole scan (`SecurityScanner.pendingKeys`, `FindingPolish.resolvePendingKeys`), independent of class order; InjuredAndroid now reports it. (2) An exported component with no manifest permission whose class checks the CALLER (getCallingPackage / getCallingUid together with signature or calling-permission APIs) is LOW with an explanation (`lowerCallerVerified`). (3) "Native method in a Serializable/Parcelable class" is HIGH only when a deserialisation hook (finalize, readObject, readResolve, createFromParcel, readFromParcel, a Parcel constructor) calls one of the class's native methods (OVAA, SQLCipher CursorWindow); a native method with parameters that no hook calls is LOW; parameterless native methods are not reported. Real-app check: K-9 HIGH to LOW, Organic Maps 2 HIGH to 0, OVAA 17 HIGH+ unchanged with the native card kept; recall kit unchanged (39 / 39, 9 / 10 probes).
  - **Known gaps left by this release**: Fossify card unread; the three failed apps; set 11 precision is not a clean number (the fixes were made on the cards of the same run, so the first-run counts above are the blind measure).

- 0.92.1 (previous):
- 0.92.1 (not built on device; Kotlin 232/232, `ir` 283/283). Version 0.92.1 / code 196. Edits in `MainActivity.kt` (not compiled here): one helper `codePackageOf(defs)` replacing three `SecurityScanner.codePackage(...)` calls. **0.92.0 WAS BUILT ON THE OWNER'S DEVICE and run on his white-label wallet app** (the owner's report, not stored in the repo): every MainActivity edit since 0.88.0 compiles and the new sections work (posture, MASVS coverage with 18 of 24 controls assessed, native split-APK card, SCA: Gson 2.8.8 from the code constant, OkHttp 4.2.2 / 2.2.0, firebase-common 20.3.1, 7 dependencyInsight lines, the control labels on the cards).
  - **The report exposed a defect bigger than the review of it said**: a white-label app (manifest package `com.TE.WEWallet`, 99 of its components and its readable code under `com.emeint.*`, a commercial obfuscator that renames most classes into the default package) had NO card of scope APP in any of the three reports (checked: all 77 cards are OBF or untagged): `SecurityScanner.codePackage` returned the manifest package. The review had said the cards had `Scope: APP` and that only the posture table used the manifest package: both wrong (posture uses codePackage like the rest). Cause not proven without the APK; the mechanism is reproduced in a unit test: when the manifest package holds at least a quarter as many classes as the readable app code (brand classes, generated R classes), the old rule picks the manifest package. **Fix**: the manifest COMPONENTS are the evidence of where the code is (`ManifestContext.componentTypes`): a package that holds at least 3 and half of the non-library components wins over the manifest package; generated `R` / `BuildConfig` / `Manifest` classes no longer count for the manifest package; components in the first two segments of the manifest package but outside the full package (Shizuku, `applicationId` suffixes like `.plus` / `.debug` / `.standalone` / `.playstore`) give the two-segment prefix.
  - **Measured by computing the code package of all 37 real apps before and after**: first version of the rule changed 2 apps, the refined one 6 (Infinity, Ivy, Kiwix, OpenTracks, Mihon, Shizuku): the four extra are apps whose `applicationId` has a suffix while the code sits in the base package: their own code was "other" and is now the app's. Full pipeline on Mihon, Shizuku, Kiwix, OpenTracks: Mihon 18 renamed-lowered cards became 22 scope APP (6 raised LOW to MEDIUM, 4 new LOW), no HIGH change anywhere; Shizuku's 8 "APP" cards were all in `rikka.shizuku` (the Shizuku-API library vendored into the app: the old code package had picked a library as the app) and are now untagged. The first version of the rule had left Shizuku with 0 APP classes: caught by running the full pipeline, not by the unit tests.
  - **Other fixes from the report (verified one by one against it)**: `net/sqlcipher/` and `com/j256/ormlite/` (+ simple-xml, zt-zip, commons-collections) added to the library prefixes (the HIGH "native method in a Serializable class" on `net.sqlcipher.CursorWindow` was a library; a test now requires every package the SCA scanner recognises to be a library for the scope classifier: only 4 of 62 were not); aggregate path groups (`androidx-libraries`, `jitpack-libraries`, `jakewharton-libraries`) no longer listed as components and no longer in the CycloneDX SBOM with a made-up purl (a note line says they are present; this was old behaviour since 0.47.1, NOT a 0.88.0 regression as the review said); `secrets-sensitive-fields` / entropy ignore, by VALUE and not by field name, a host + path without a scheme (Tink `type.googleapis.com/google.crypto.tink...`), a regular expression (`(?i)...`) and a comma list of identifiers (the Facebook SDK response types; the exact values were not in the report, only their length and first characters).
  - **NOT fixed**: the Jackson classes that the obfuscator renamed into the default package (cards 9 - 14, 18, MEDIUM, scope OBF): they cannot be told from app code by name; a content fingerprint of known library classes would be needed. The posture class `Mwallet2ApplicationClassResetPasswordMode` (default package, renamed) stays "origin unknown" unless it refers to the app's package three times.
  - **Mutation check**: components rule off, value filters removed, aggregates kept: 4 tests fail. Recall kit unchanged.
  - **Next**: the owner's wallet app should be rescanned with 0.92.1: the app's own cards should now carry `Scope: APP`, the posture table should list `SecuredActivity` as app code; then the attack-scenario summary, the API-capability inventory, the missing MobSF rules.

Every hit has an ORIGIN: app code (under the app's code package, or a renamed class that refers to it), unknown origin (renamed without a reference to the app, or a readable class outside the app's package), or library only.

Two wiring mistakes of mine: the first hook sat in `scanSmali`, which a single-class AI review also calls (it would have counted a class twice); and `clsAppOnly` is a Classes-tab view option, not a scan filter. One DOUBT that was wrong: Syncthing-Fork's emulator hit looked like a false positive (my grep found one emulator string) but the class has two (`sdk_gphone_x86_arm`, `Android SDK built for x86`): the detector was right.

Full pipeline on Allsafe: the section renders (pinning challenge, RootBeer call, FLAG_SECURE in MainActivity; `androidx.emoji2` reading signatures shows as "library only (12)"). Recall kit unchanged (39 / 39 must, 9 / 10 probes). Mutation check (every hit app code, substring frida, FLAG_SECURE on any addFlags): 4 tests fail.

- 0.90.0 (previous):
- 0.90.0 (not built on device; Kotlin 207/207, `ir` 283/283). Version 0.90.0 / code 193. **No edit in an Android-only file this time** (`ElfAnalyzer.kt`, `NativeScanner.kt` and a hook in `ScanRulesResources.kt`, all compile in the sandbox). Stage 2 of the plan from the competitor research (native code, the biggest gap: it was a single "not analysed" card). Details and numbers: `tools/native/README.md`.
  - **`ElfAnalyzer`**: pure Kotlin ELF32 / ELF64 reader: program headers, dynamic section, dynamic symbols from `.dynsym` (hash tables when section headers are stripped), data-section strings. **Validated against `readelf` on 225 real libraries (222 ELF) of the 37 APKs: 13 fields each, 2,886 comparisons, 0 differences** (3 files are zip archives named .so, rejected by both). One false alarm on the way was the oracle's own regex (hex symbol sizes), not the reader.
  - **`NativeScanner`** (replaces the 0.82.0 "not analysed" card whenever there are ELF libraries): one card per concern, never per library: 16 KB page alignment (MEDIUM when the target SDK >= 35, the Google Play rule; INFO below), real hardening defects (executable stack, TEXTREL, no RELRO, an RPATH that others can write: MEDIUM for an unclassified library, LOW for a known dependency), private-directory RPATH (INFO), unsafe C imports (LOW, only libraries that are not a known generic dependency), credentials in the data strings (the 206 vendor formats, HIGH; PEM private key CRITICAL) and an INFO coverage card with the counts (no canary / no FORTIFY / unaligned) and the statement that the native CODE is not analysed.
  - **Calibration from the real libraries** (100 arm64): NX 100%, full RELRO 100%, canary 78%, unsafe imports 17%, 16 KB aligned 83%. So the classic flags almost never fire and a missing canary or a strcpy import is normal in a bundled library: that is why they are in the summary card and not cards of their own, as an outside review suggested.
  - **Two mistakes of mine caught by looking at the results**: the first target-SDK assumption (35 for every app) made 3 apps look non-compliant that target 28 / 31 / 34 (now read from each manifest); and the list of "known third-party libraries" first held names of libraries specific to apps of my corpus (termux, aria2c, zcash ...): over-fitting, removed: only generic libraries remain, an app-specific name counts as the app's own. A 3rd item: the first RPATH rule made a private-directory RUNPATH (`/data/data/com.termux/files/usr/lib` in Seal's libraries) a MEDIUM defect; it is information now.

- 0.86.0 (previous):
- 0.86.0 (not built on device; Kotlin 164/164 in the sandbox (156 + 8 in `SecretsInversion0860Test`), `ir` 281/281 unchanged (the ir package was not touched). Version 0.86.0 / code 186. **Touches NO Android-only file** (new `SecretEvidence.kt`, `ScanRulesLegacy.kt`, `SecurityScanner.kt`, tests, version). Proposed by the outside review of 0.85.2 ("invert the secret logic: HIGH only with positive evidence"), agreed with the owner.
  - **Secret detectors need positive evidence for HIGH** (`SecretEvidence`): the value reaches a secret sink IN THE SAME METHOD (a KeySpec / IV / GCM spec, Cipher or Mac init, a KeyStore password, okhttp `Credentials.basic` / `PasswordAuthentication` / `setBasicAuth`, a JWT signer library, an `Authorization` / API-key header with a header call, a query parameter whose name ends in token / key / secret / appid / auth with a query-parameter call), or it has a credential FORMAT the known-format rules do not cover (Laravel Sanctum `id|40 alphanumerics`). For a field, the methods that read it and the methods holding the same literal (inlined constants) are checked. `secrets-sensitive-fields`: evidence + strong shape = HIGH, one of them = MEDIUM, neither = LOW (confidence MEDIUM without evidence); `high-entropy-strings` near a credential word: evidence = HIGH, else MEDIUM. The card says which evidence it saw, or that there was none. Known-format rules (JWT, AWS, Google, PEM ...) are unchanged. Evidence is CO-OCCURRENCE in one method, not data flow.
  - **Tests** with negative twins (sink in another method = MEDIUM; a header name alone, a `Log` call, an ordinary query name `lang`, a 41-char or dashed Sanctum-like value are not evidence). Mutation check (evidence always null): the 4 positive tests of the first version fail, the negatives pass; NOT repeated after the two follow-ups below.
  - **Found while measuring, fixed before closing** (both are recall costs of "same method only"): Breezy `uu` (a weather provider key `1|...40`, judged a real embedded key in 0.82.0) fell to MEDIUM -> the Sanctum format; Allsafe `NoteReceiver` (a hardcoded `auth_token` query value, the app's intended hardcoded-credential case) fell to MEDIUM -> query names ending in token / key / secret / appid / auth. More of this shape are EXPECTED (a key passed to a Retrofit annotation or added by an interceptor in another class): the recall-by-injection kit is the next step.
  - **Measured (31 APKs, reach-aware sandbox, batched; vs 0.85.2)**: benchmark recall unchanged (Sieve 8/8, Allsafe 6/7, PIVAA 21/25, Vuldroid 7/8, OVAA 16/19); HIGH / CRITICAL cards 90 -> 90 (no HIGH added or lost: every judged secret TP stays HIGH - Breezy 3 JWT + `uu`, Monerujo JWT, Read You DES key (its key use is seen), Allsafe `auth_token`); 5 MEDIUM cards became LOW (Nextcloud Notes `PRIVATE_KEY` / `PUSH_TOKEN`, Markor `TEXT_TOKEN` / `HTML_TOKEN_ITEM_E...`, Mihon ShikimoriApi entropy) and one library LOW left the report (Breezy `io.jsonwebtoken` `GENERIC_SECRET_ALG_PREFIX`). Honest reading: on these 31 already-tuned apps the change removes no HIGH, because the old FP families were already filtered by name; its value is on NEW apps, to be shown by the next blind set.

Version 0.85.1 / code 184. **0.85.0 was built on the owner's device with no compile errors (owner, 2026-10-16)**, so `MainActivity.kt` edits up to 0.85.0 compile; 0.85.1 touches NO Android-only file (Axml.kt, new AxmlAttrIds.kt, tests, version).
  - **Origin**: an outside review (Gemini, read only BLUEPRINT/HANDOFF) said the 14 unnamed attributes of InstaPay (`attr_10104f6`, `attr_10103af`) were autoVerify / fullBackupContent / dataExtractionRules / roundIcon. Checked against AOSP `public-final.xml`: `0x010104f6` = resizeableActivity and `0x010103af` = supportsRtl (not security attributes); the real ids are autoVerify 0x010104ee, fullBackupContent 0x010104eb, dataExtractionRules 0x0101063e, roundIcon 0x0101052c. Its networkSecurityConfig worry was wrong (0x01010527 was already in the 0.85.0 table). Its VALIDATION vs `cleansFileName` remark is true but is the documented 0.84.0 decision (names can lie), not a new finding.
  - **Real gap found while checking** (0.85.0 table = first 45 attributes + 6 by hand): the manifest rules search these names as TEXT and none was in the table: `android:autoVerify` (ManifestDeepLinksRule: a stripped manifest with a verified App Link got a false LOW "No android:autoVerify"), `android:testOnly` (ManifestTestOnlyRule: missed), `android:requestLegacyExternalStorage` (missed). Also a WRONG entry: `0x010103d1` was mapped to usesCleartextTraffic but is `indicatorStart` (the real one, 0x010104ec, was already there; nobody documented where the wrong one came from).
  - **Fix**: `AxmlAttrIds.kt` holds ALL 1571 framework attribute names (android.R.attr, ids 0x0101xxxx), GENERATED from AOSP `public-final.xml`, not typed by hand; `Axml.knownAttrs` reads it (lazy). Used only when the string pool has no name, so a manifest that keeps its names decodes exactly as before. The hand table (45 + 6) is gone; the 0.85.0 test (`manifestAttributesWithoutPoolNamesAreStillNamed`) still passes.
  - **Tests** (`AxmlStrippedNamesTest`, a synthetic binary manifest with an all-empty attribute-name pool + resource map, built in the test): the ids above; InstaPay's two unknown ids are NOT security attributes; `0x010103d1` is indicatorStart; non-framework ids stay unnamed; decode gives `android:name / autoVerify / testOnly / scheme` and no `attr_`; `ManifestDeepLinksRule` gives INFO (no "No android:autoVerify") with autoVerify and LOW without it (negative twin); `ManifestTestOnlyRule` fires. Mutation check: with the table disabled the new tests fail (see the line below).
  - **NOT measured**: no real APK was re-scanned (the sandbox has no APKs; InstaPay / Axis Pay are not in it). Expected effect on InstaPay: the 14 `attr_*` become named; whether any rule card changes there is unknown. Set 9 stays closed (owner, 2026-10-17 entry below).
  - **Open (from the same review, not done)**: (1) provenance of `SsaBridge.VALIDATION` is still unknown (see the 0.83.0 note); (2) a device check of 0.85.1 and a blind set 10 of open-source apps (protocol: `heldout_real/PROTOCOL*.md`).

Run BEFORE the library-prefix edit (that one only changes scope labels; the two set-9 apps are re-scanned below).

  - **Axml defect fixed (found on InstaPay's manifest)**: when a manifest's attribute names are stripped from the string pool (resource-name shrinking), `Axml.decode` printed `attr_1010003="com.x.Receiver"` instead of `android:name=...`, so every component was "(unnamed)", the manifest rules and the component list used for the SSA entries were blind, and `allowBackup="false"` was not seen (a false "Backup allowed" card). `Axml.knownAttrs` now holds the first 45 framework attributes in `android.R.attr` order (theme .. pathPattern: name = 0x01010003, permission = 0x01010006, exported = 0x01010010, authorities = 0x01010018, grantUriPermissions = 0x0101001b, scheme / host ...) plus targetActivity, minSdk, targetSdk, allowBackup, versionCode / Name, networkSecurityConfig, usesCleartextTraffic (0x010104ec); fallback names carry the `android:` prefix the rules search for. `Axml.fallbackName` is the test hook. Measured on 31 reports: only InstaPay changed (8 "(unnamed)" cards named, the false allowBackup card gone); benchmark recall unchanged. Attributes still unnamed in that manifest: 14 (e.g. `attr_10104f6`, `attr_10103af`), meaning unknown. Kotlin 140/140.

- 0.84.0 (previous):
- 0.84.0 (not built on device; `ir` 281/281, Kotlin 135/135 in the sandbox). Version 0.84.0 / code 182. **0.83.0 was built on the owner's device with NO compile errors** (so every unverified `MainActivity.kt` edit from 0.75.2 to 0.83.0 compiles; their behaviour, e.g. the AI-triage prompt, is still not seen). Edits in `MainActivity.kt` of 0.84.0 (not compiled): `FindingPolish.lowerNonExportedProviders(secFindings, nonExportedProviders(mc) { superclass lookup over classDefs })` in `reachPass`. **Fixes from real-app set 8** (Quillpad, OpenTracks, Orbot: first run on 0.83.0 = 1 TP-low / 5 HIGH cards + 1 false negative, `heldout_real/PROTOCOL_SET8.md`). Set 8 is a tuning set now.
  - **False negative closed**: `tls-trustmanager` skips what R8 / Kotlin put before `return-void`: `Object.getClass()` null checks, `nop`, `.line` / `.local` directives, calls to `kotlin.jvm.internal.Intrinsics` taking (Object[, String]) (`SecurityScanner.NULL_CHECKS`). A real check (any other call) keeps the manager out of the pattern. Quillpad's trust-all `gq2` is reported (HIGH after the OBF lowering; CRITICAL would be right).
  - **Providers the manifest does not export**: (1) the SSA source "a ContentProvider method parameter" is internal / weak when the provider is not an exported entry of the graph (`CallGraph.isExportedEntry`, only when a component list exists) and the card goes one more level down (`SsaBridge`, sentence about grantUriPermissions); (2) `FindingPolish.lowerNonExportedProviders` lowers the text rules `provider-sql-injection` / `provider-openfile-segment` for a provider the manifest declares exported=false AND for its app-class superclass chain (OpenTracks declares `ShareContentProvider`, the rule fires in `CustomContentProvider`), minus classes shared with an exported provider (`nonExportedProviders(mc, superOf)`).
  - **dedupe no longer raises a lowered SSA card**: an in-class taint twin (old engine, no manifest knowledge) used to give the merged card its HIGH ("higher severity wins"); now a twin of the same class and title does not raise an SSA taint card whose confidence is LOW.
  - **File names that lose their separators** (`SsaBridge.cleansFileName`, file sinks only): a method of the path, or a String-returning app callee one call down, that takes the base name (`lastIndexOf(sep)` + `substring(int)`) or `replace(char, char)` of the separator, where the separator is the constant '/' or `File.separatorChar`: confidence LOW, one level lower, with a sentence. A helper only NAMED sanitize* keeps the one-step drop (a test from an unknown writer, see the 0.83.0 provenance note, fixes that on purpose).
  - **Measured (sandbox, reach-aware, batched)**: all 26 apps on the 0.84.0 snapshot: only Quillpad (1 -> 2), OpenTracks (2 -> 1) and Orbot (2 -> 0) changed; the other 23 identical to 0.83.0; recall unchanged (Sieve 8/8, Allsafe 6/7, PIVAA 21/25, Vuldroid 7/8, OVAA 16/19). After the callee and separator fixes the 16 apps with file-path HIGH cards and the benchmarks were re-run: exactly one card changed (OpenTracks KMZ HIGH -> MEDIUM), no judged TP lowered. NOT re-run with the final ir: andotp, aps, monerujo, davx5, kiwix, ivy, jellyfin, seal, shizuku, tusky, libretube, catima, mihon (the last two fixes only touch file-sink flows).
  - **Method note**: `tools/sandbox/batch.sh <tag> <snapshotdir> apps...` runs resumable batches from a COPY of the compiled classes (rebuilding the working classes while a loop runs killed earlier runs; the environment also pauses between replies, so every batch skips apps whose report exists). `tools/sandbox/ItemDump.java` prints SsaBridge items of a whole app.
  - Not done: Quillpad `gq2` severity (the OBF "likely third-party" lowering still applies to tiny renamed classes without app references: a trust-all manager is never "just a library"), set 9 on 0.84.0, a device build of 0.84.0.
- 0.83.0 (previous):
- 0.83.0 (unverified on device; `ir` 276/276, Kotlin 129/129 in the sandbox; edits in `MainActivity.kt` (not compiled here): `f.scope = scopeOfText(...)` for SSA cards, plus the 0.82.0 AI-triage part and the 0.75.2 - 0.82.0 reachPass lines, all still never built after 0.80.0). Version 0.83.0 / code 181. **Fixes from real-app set 7** (Tusky, FairEmail, Shizuku: first run 4 TP / 19 HIGH cards, `heldout_real/PROTOCOL_SET7.md`). Set 7 is a tuning set now.
  - **Weak taint**, a family of structural tests instead of name tests (a weak flow shows as LOW confidence, one level lower, with a sentence saying why): (1) the RESULT of a call that resolves to `FANOUT_WEAK` = 8 or more targets (via marked `fanout:`; FairEmail `SimpleTask.onExecute`); (2) a chain with `ERASED_WEAK` = 3 or more type-erased methods (every reference parameter and the return are java.lang.Object; `TaintIr.erasedHops`; via `erased:`; AnkiDroid coroutine glue); (3) a Parcel-sourced value that arrives through a field or a callee result or a chain of more than one method (via `parcelheld:`); used right where it is read (OVAA MemoryCorruptionParcelable) it stays strong. Mutation-checked only for (1).
  - **Library code repackaged into the app's package** (`SecurityScanner.isRepackagedLibrary` / `scopeOfText`): class name of <= 3 characters + a library marker string (androidx fragment / lifecycle / savedstate / ..., kotlinx, okhttp3, retrofit2, "Unable to instantiate fragment", META-INF/services) + fewer than 3 references to readable classes of the app's package = LIB. Used by `RuleContext.scope`, `scanSmali` and the SSA cards. NOT a package-name test: markers are androidx / Kotlin strings that every app carries. Shizuku's two Class.forName cards were androidx FragmentFactory / savedstate.
  - **Activity-result helpers by shape** (`(int, int, Intent)` in the descriptor, whatever the name; R8 renames `onActivityResult` helpers). **Libraries**: `me/leolin/shortcutbadger/`, `net/openid/appauth/`.
  - **Sandbox fidelity**: `tools/sandbox/SandboxScanReach.kt` sets the reach label of each SSA card and runs `lowerUnreachedHelpers` (the sandbox had neither before, so earlier HIGH counts of sets 2-7 were slightly high for helper-class flows; set-7 counts did not change with it). Use it from now on; keep `SandboxScan.kt` only to compare with old rows.
  - **Measured (26 apps, reach-aware sandbox)**: benchmark recall unchanged (Sieve 8/8, Allsafe 6/7, PIVAA 21/25, Vuldroid 7/8, OVAA 16/19 found with reach, 14/19 without); HIGH/CRIT: Shizuku 2 -> 0, FairEmail 19 -> 7, Kiwix 1 -> 0, Ivy 1 -> 0, Termux 7 -> 6, Breezy 5 -> 4, AnkiDroid 1 -> 1 (different card: the k73 FP gone, `CardContentProvider.insert` -> file write now visible), all other apps equal. Every drop I read was a judged FP (Termux `ExecutionCommand.toString`, Breezy objectbox, Kiwix loadUrl, Ivy glance receiver, Shizuku androidx); all judged TPs are still HIGH (Termux FileReceiverActivity, Read You DES + trust-all, Breezy 3 JWT, Monerujo JWT, Infinity WebView, Markor `_data`, Jellyfin bridge, InsecureShop 9).
  - **Known risk**: the erased-hops and fan-out tests can also weaken a TRUE flow in a coroutine / callback heavy app (measured only by HIGH counts on 26 apps). A weakened flow is still reported, one level lower. **A displaced flow can surface**: weakening a flow can let another, stronger source reach the same sink (AnkiDroid `w87`), so a version can ADD cards.
  - **Provenance note**: the pattern in `SsaBridge.VALIDATION` for app methods whose NAME says they clean / check a value (`sanitiz|normaliz|canonical|escape|validat|isSafe|isValid|checkPath|safeName`, comment "0.83.0 (FairEmail Helper.sanitizeFilename ...)") was in the working tree when I looked at the file; I did not write it and do not know who did. It compiles, the tests pass, and it only lowers CONFIDENCE (validation never lowers severity), which is why FairEmail `ActivityBase.processUri` stays HIGH although it calls `Helper.sanitizeFilename`.
  - Not done: the 2 FairEmail `file path` cards left (one has a sanitizer in the path: make a recognised sanitizer lower the severity, measure it on the 26 apps); a device build; set 8 (new unseen apps).
- 0.82.0 (previous):
- 0.82.0 (unverified on device; Kotlin 125/125 in the sandbox, `ir` unchanged; MainActivity: the SSA findings pass `appBound` to `FindingPolish.apply` (one changed call)). Version 0.82.0 / code 180. **From the JustMarkets precision read** (`tools/benchmark/heldout_real/JUSTMARKETS_JUDGED.md`: "0 HIGH" was honest for 6.15.1, but the obfuscation lowering fired on ~6 cards of the app's OWN renamed code).
  - **Renamed app code is app code** (`SecurityScanner.isAppBoundObf`, used by `scanSmali` and the SSA findings): an OBF-scope class that references the app package >= 3 times and has no SDK identity string becomes scope APP and skips every OBF lowering (the card says "Renamed by R8, but this is the app's own code"). Mixed R8-merged classes (Ivy `I4/n` = Crashlytics + com.ivy entities) stay OBF.
  - **JVM descriptors are not secrets** (`looksLikeName`: `getAccessToken()Ljava/lang/String;`, `setTimeout(J)V`).
  - **Native coverage card** (`nativeCoverageFinding`, resource pass, LOW, rule `native-coverage`): "Native code not in this APK (not analysed)" when the dex loads libraries / the manifest declares an ABI split but there is no lib/*.so (JustMarkets), else "Native libraries not analysed (N .so)".
  - **Report scope line** (`ReportBuilder.securitySection`): business logic, server-side bugs, native code and cross-group flows are said to be outside the scan.
  - Measured: JustMarkets mu.j / st.d / cq.a attributed, mu.h FP gone, native card present; Breezy +1 HIGH TP (embedded `1|...` API token, was lowered); AnkiDroid +10 MEDIUM, Infinity +4 MEDIUM (renamed app code, not all read); Ivy unchanged; benchmarks unchanged.
  - **Still open from earlier versions (not done, reasons)**: Kiwix origin pinned by a constant prefix (needs a "fixed origin" value model in TaintIr); taint through METHODS of renamed library classes (Breezy `o02.next()`: TaintIr has no app/library attribution yet; the new isAppBoundObf could feed it); Termux object sensitivity (measured: ~1 FP, postponed); a trust-all TrustManager behind an opt-in flag (Read You: the flag is in another class); Jellyfin bridge through an event bus (cross-class); AnkiDroid PreferencesActivity (non-exported, main process: kept HIGH on purpose).
  - **AI triage context (second part of 0.82.0, added after the first part above was already in the tree)**: a taint flow was judged from the SINK method only (`Council.checkFinding` and the triage prompt got the card text cut at 400 chars + the enclosing method of the sink), so the method where the untrusted value is read and the path were invisible. Now `SsaBridge.Item` carries `sourceMethod` (chain head, "" when source and sink are in one method) and `dataPath` ("source -> ... -> sink", short names, at most 12 steps); `Finding.sourceMethod / dataPath` hold them (set in `MainActivity.ssaFindings`); `triageWithAi` appends "Data path" and the code of the source method (`SecurityScanner.methodExcerpt`, 1800 chars, cut at a line end) to the code excerpt, and "Why flagged" is cut at 900 instead of 400 for such findings (`Council.checkFinding` too). The "explain this finding" prompt is unchanged. Tests: `IrItemPathTest` (2), `methodExcerptFindsTheMethodByKeyAndCutsAtALineEnd`. NOT verified end to end: no AI call can be made here and the `MainActivity` change is not compiled in the sandbox.
  - **Provenance note**: the first part of this version (app-bound renamed classes, descriptor literals, native coverage card, report scope line, `JustMarketsFixes0820Test`, version 0.82.0 / code 180) was already in the working tree when the AI-triage part was started; it was reviewed, compiled and its tests run (Kotlin 126/126, `ir` 268/268 with the new tests), and re-measured: JustMarkets code rules 0.81.0 -> 0.82.0: mu.j (MEDIUM, plaintext tokens) / st.d / cq.a (OkHttp logs bodies, LOW) attributed to the app and no longer lowered, mu.h entropy FP gone, native card present (73 cards, was 72); benchmarks unchanged (Sieve 8/8, Allsafe 6/7, PIVAA 21/25, Vuldroid 7/8, OVAA 19/19 present, HIGH counts equal); Breezy +1 HIGH (embedded API token, the one that had been lowered); AnkiDroid HIGH 1 -> 1. Limit seen: a TINY renamed class has no app references to attribute by (JustMarkets `ar.c`, the always-true hostname verifier, stays OBF); it is MEDIUM anyway and it is the trading socket's pinning kill-switch (see JUSTMARKETS_JUDGED.md).
  - **Measurement round (no code change; `tools/benchmark/MEASUREMENTS_0820.md`, tool `tools/sandbox/MeasureCompare.java`)**: grouping loses ~9% of AnkiDroid's SSA sinks at the device group size 3000 (3 of 34, all SharedPreferences-sourced; none Intent-sourced; 0 lost on Termux / Markor / Jellyfin) and 35% at 1000; grouping also adds a few flows the whole-app run lacks. The old in-class taint engine contributes 1 card of 123 taint cards over 23 apps (Catima provider SQL, BY-DESIGN). So neither boundary summaries nor an engine merge is a measured recall win; JustMarkets itself cannot be run whole in 4 GB (its loss is not measured).

  (kept below) 0.81.0 (unverified on device; Kotlin 121/121 in the sandbox, `ir` unchanged (266/266); 3 more lines in `MainActivity.reachPass` after the 0.80.0 one: `lowerSeparateProcessDialogs`, `applyProviderCodeChecks`, `applyNetworkEvidence`). Version 0.81.0 / code 179. **0.80.0 built and ran on the owner's device** (JustMarkets 6.15.1: Deep scan finished 59667/59667 classes, SSA +11 flows, plan 46 s + groups 170 s on 2 threads, 0 HIGH / 30 MEDIUM / 53 LOW listed; report in `tools/benchmark/runs/justmarkets-6.15.1-0.80.0-device.md`; not judged, no APK here). Fixes from real-app set 6 and the network cards of sets 4-6:
  - **Network configuration severity**: `manifest-cleartext-traffic` and `res-nsc-cleartext` are MEDIUM by default; `FindingPolish.applyNetworkEvidence` raises them to HIGH when the new rule **cleartext-url-used** (`CleartextUrlUsedRule`, APP scope) finds a `const-string "http://<host>"` in a method that also opens a connection / builds a request / loads a page (URL, HttpURLConnection, OkHttp, Retrofit baseUrl, WebView.loadUrl, Volley, Ktor; local and documentation hosts ignored). `res-nsc-user-ca` is MEDIUM (every domain) / LOW (some domains). No package-name list (decided against: it would tune the scanner to the apps already seen).
  - **Provider permission in code**: new INFO marker rule **provider-code-permission** (`ProviderCodePermissionRule`) when EVERY overridden data method (query, insert, update, delete, bulkInsert, openFile, call) checks the caller in its body or in a same-class method it calls (2 hops): check/enforce Calling(OrSelf)Permission, Calling(Uri)Permission, getCallingPackage / getCallingUid. `FindingPolish.applyProviderCodeChecks` then lowers "Exported provider without a permission: X" to LOW (idempotent). One open method = no marker.
  - **Separate-process dialogs**: `FindingPolish.lowerSeparateProcessDialogs`: Intent taint whose chain / via names a NOT exported activity declared with `android:process` (ACRA's crash dialog) drops one level. Non-exported activities in the main process keep their severity (OVAA / Infinity forwards).
  - **JS bridge helpers**: `JsBridgeMethodRule.dangerousExposed` follows calls to methods of the SAME class up to 2 hops and names the helper. It does not cross classes (Jellyfin `openUrl` goes through an event bus in another class: still named only via hideMediaSession).
  - Measured (sandbox, 23 apps): benchmarks keep their recall (Sieve 8/8, Allsafe 6/7, PIVAA 21/25, Vuldroid 7/8, OVAA 19/19 present); HIGH/CRIT: AnkiDroid 6 -> 1, Jellyfin 3 -> 1, LibreTube 2 -> 0, Mihon 2 -> 0, DAVx5 2 -> 0, Read You 3 -> 2, Infinity 2 -> 1, Markor 3 -> 2, Monerujo 2 -> 1, Ivy 2 -> 1, Vuldroid 9 -> 8 and InsecureShop 10 -> 9 (their cleartext cards: neither has an http:// literal in its own code). Every TP card judged in sets 1-6 is still HIGH except Ivy's cleartext (judged "TP-config, severity overstated").
  - Held-out: set 6 is a tuning set now; held-out = Sieve only. Next: set 7 on 0.81.0; a device check of 0.81.0 (the 3 new reachPass lines).

Version 0.80.0 / code 178. **Fixes measured on real-app sets 4 + 5** (both 0.79.0, unseen apps: precision 6/10 by card, 3/6 by issue; heldout_real/PROTOCOL_SET4.md, PROTOCOL_SET5.md). - 0.79.0 (previous):
  (kept below) 0.79.0 (unverified on device; `ir` 265/265, Kotlin 112/112 in the sandbox; no UI change). Version 0.79.0 / code 177.
  - **One root cause = one card** (`SsaBridge.newFlows`): sink groups whose head flow has the same sink kind, source, first chain method (where the input is read), reachability and strength become one card listing the other sink methods ("one root cause, one card"). Markor 2 cards -> 1, Termux 1 merge. Groups whose source is read in the sink method itself are never merged.
  - **onActivityResult helpers** (`TaintIr.isActivityResultIntent`): a method whose name contains "ActivityResult" and whose parameters end with `(..., int, int, Intent)` is treated like onActivityResult (Markor `GsContextUtils.extractResultFromActivityResult`): its Intent is the answer of an activity the app started.
  - **Library**: `com/tonyodev/fetch2` (Kiwix download library).
  - **Measurement tool** `tools/sandbox/FlowDump.java` (every TaintIr flow with source / via / chain, non-library classes, entries = exported components). **Field-conflation census** (see heldout_real/PROTOCOL_SET3.md): 56 flows on 8 real apps, 14 through a heap field, only 1 of the judged HIGH FPs is caused by field conflation, so the object-sensitive heap (planned 0.80.0) is NOT worth its memory cost now.

Same literal key on both sides stays strong; `getAll` stays sound. Markor's preferences wrapper (resource-id keys) no longer turns every read into the caller's path. `IrPrefsHeapTest`: 2 expectations changed on purpose (non-literal key -> SharedPreferences lead), new `IrPrefsKeyTest`.

  - **Secret detectors vs public values** (`SecurityScanner.looksLikeName`, used by high-entropy-strings and secrets-sensitive-fields): Kotlin null-check messages `name(...)`, call / ABI signatures `safeTransferFrom(address,uint256)`, 32-44 char base58 strings (public keys / addresses / program ids; Solana secret keys 87-88 and Bitcoin WIF 51-52 are still checked), identifiers made of words (`index_TokenTransfer_transactionHash`, `averagePrice7d_tokenQueryId`; `identifierLike`: >= 3 word parts, mostly >= 3 letters, vowels >= 25%, no random 2-letter chunks; a Stripe-like key stays a secret, test).
