  - **PEM**: "Private key block" / "Private key embedded" (code, AndroGoat, resources) need the base64 body after the header in the same literal; a header alone is what key parsers contain.
  - **crypto-cipher**: `RSA/ECB/...` (and EC, ElGamal) is not "ECB"; RSA with PKCS#1 v1.5 stays the LOW card.
  - **crypto-hardcoded-key**: BIP32 / SLIP-0010 master-key HMAC keys ("Bitcoin seed", "ed25519 seed", "Nist256p1 seed", "curve25519 seed") are spec constants.
  - **fintech-unencrypted-storage**: a class that never opens a preferences file itself (no getSharedPreferences / getDefaultSharedPreferences) gets its SharedPreferences from outside (Hilt injected EncryptedSharedPreferences in Password Store): LOW with a "check where it is created" note, confidence LOW.
  - **Engine, Kotlin `by lazy { }`** (CallGraph.addLazyEdges + kotlin/Lazy, UnsafeLazyImpl, SynchronizedLazyImpl as containers in TaintIr BUILDERS): a static call shaped `(...Lkotlin/jvm/functions/Function0;)Lkotlin/Lazy;` (R8 renames the helper, Password Store has `Lkotlin/ExceptionsKt;->unsafeLazy`) is linked to `<lambda type>->invoke()Ljava/lang/Object;` (the new-instance / INSTANCE that defines the last argument register, 12 instructions back), so the site gets the lambda's return taint and `Lazy.getValue()` returns it. Edges go to the main graph for reachable callers (no new reachability) or the taint-only maps. Recovers the Password Store `DecryptActivity` FILE_PATH flow without analysing kotlin stdlib (the device never analyses library classes). Tests `IrLazyTest` (3; the mutation without the edges fails them).
  - **Sandbox fidelity**: `tools/sandbox/SandboxScan.kt` now leaves library classes out of the SSA like `MainActivity.ssaFindings` (flag `withlibs` for the old behaviour, `nossa` for apps too big for the sandbox). - 0.75.1 (previous):
  (kept below) 0.75.1 (unverified on device; `ir` 252/252, Kotlin 95/95 in the sandbox). Version 0.75.1 / code 172.
  - **Sieve blind run (0.75.0, sandbox)**: recall 8/8, 1 extra CRITICAL = true positive (see `tools/benchmark/HELDOUT_RESULTS.md`). New tool `tools/sandbox/SandboxScan.kt` (the Kotlin pipeline without the UI -> a bench.py-compatible report). Held-out set: Allsafe + Sieve.
  - **weak-random rewritten on the IR (`IrRules.weakRandom`, same id)**: the OUTPUT of java.util.Random / Math.random is followed forward in SSA (moves, arithmetic, conversions, String / StringBuilder / Base64 / BigInteger, arrays via nextBytes / aput) to a security use: a key / IV / GCM / PBE spec, a field / ContentValues / prefs / extra named like a secret, a call to a same-class method named like one, or the RETURN of a method / class named like one or of a class that uses javax.crypto / MessageDigest. The receiver must be traced to `new java.util.Random` (a SecureRandom held as Random, a field or a parameter = quiet). `new Random(<const>)` = HIGH. One finding per method. The old "security word nearby" text rule is `legacy()` (parser failure only). Designed from PIVAA's `Encryption.rng()` miss (PIVAA = tuning set).
  - **Measured**: PIVAA 0 -> 1 (Encryption.rng), Allsafe 0 -> 1 (`WeakCryptography.randomNumber`, a held-out app: not tuned on it), Vuldroid, OVAA, InsecureShop, NewPipe, Sieve 0 (legacy 0 everywhere too).

- 0.75.0 (previous):
  (kept below) 0.75.0 (unverified on device; `ir` 248/248 and Kotlin non-android tests 95/95 in the sandbox; `MainActivity.kt` NOT touched, NOT compiled). Version 0.75.0 / code 171. **Four rules decided on the IR** (owner's spec: context-aware, not regex).
  - **Integration (no state explosion)**: new `ir/IrRules.java` works on ONE parsed class; `RuleContext.ir` (lazy, parsed at most once per class, only when a rule's trigger API is in the text); SSA built only for methods that contain the trigger invoke; every trace has a depth cap (24) and a visited set; no global or cross-scan state. TaintIr / CallGraph / ScopePlanner untouched (the taint flows are byte-identical to 0.74.0). Tapjacking runs in the resource pass with `ApkFacts` (minSdk + one streaming pass over classes*.dex for the mitigation API names, 64 KB buffer, nothing kept).
  - **broadcast-unprotected-send (rewritten, same id) + implicit-service-intent**: the Intent argument of sendBroadcast / sendOrderedBroadcast / sendStickyBroadcast / ...AsUser / startService / startForegroundService / bindService is traced back in SSA (moves, casts, phis, Intent builders that return `this`) to `new-instance Intent`; all aliases of that object are collected; it is flagged only if no setPackage / setComponent / setClass / setClassName (setPackage(null) does not count), no `(Context, Class)` / ComponentName constructor, the permission argument is absent or the const null, and the object never reaches another method / field / array (escape = quiet). Copy constructor, parameter, field, call result = quiet. LocalBroadcastManager and platform actions without private extras are skipped. Severity: private extra key or action name HIGH, own extras MEDIUM, none LOW; services one step lower (implicit service Intents throw since API 21). The 0.48 text rule is kept as `legacy()` and runs only if the IR parser fails. **Vuldroid EmailViewer (`com.example.Broadcast`, no `.action.` in the name) is now found: so Vuldroid moved to the tuning set.**
  - **sqlite-unencrypted (CWE-312)**: a sensitive column (const key, the NAME of a constant field such as COLUMN_PASSWORD, or a column in a constant CREATE TABLE / INSERT text) or a PII source (AccountManager.getPassword / peekAuthToken, TelephonyManager.getDeviceId / getImei / getSubscriberId / getLine1Number / getSimSerialNumber, Location lat/long) reaching ContentValues.put / execSQL / SQLiteStatement.bind* through an SSA backward slice (moves, phis, StringBuilder / String / boxing calls). A hash / cipher / KDF call on the slice = quiet. SQLCipher / requery classes in the class = quiet. Otherwise one INFO per SQLiteOpenHelper (hygiene). The value is NOT followed into other methods: the TaintIr lattice models attacker input, not confidentiality; adding PII sources there would mix the two (it needs a second label, not done).
  - **webview-plugin-state (CWE-477)**: setPluginState argument traced to `sget-object PluginState->ON / ON_DEMAND` (or `PluginState.valueOf("ON")`) through moves and phis; ON on any phi operand = "on at least one path"; setPluginsEnabled(true) by constant. LOW (ON_DEMAND INFO): plugins are unsupported since 4.3.
  - **tapjacking-sensitive-layout (CWE-1021, resource rule)**: a res layout with a password EditText (inputType class text + variation password / visible / web password, number password, or password="true") and no filterTouchesWhenObscured="true" on any view in the file (a parent covers its subtree). MEDIUM; LOW if the dex references setFilterTouchesWhenObscured / onFilterTouchEventForSecurity / setHideOverlayWindows anywhere; INFO if minSdk >= 31. Library layout names (abc_, mtrl_, design_ ...) skipped. Axml now names attr ids 0x010102c4 (filterTouchesWhenObscured) and 0x0101020c (minSdkVersion) when the pool has no names. NOT done: mapping a layout to its Activity (needs the arsc id -> name table; R8 inlines and strips R$layout), so the code cross-check is APK-wide.
  - **Measured (sandbox, real Kotlin rules + real resource pipeline)**: Vuldroid broadcast LOW (EmailViewer) + 2 tapjacking; PIVAA plugin-state LOW, broadcast HIGH (`service.vulnerable.vulnerableservice.LOG`), SQLite INFO (columns title/author), 1 tapjacking; Allsafe SQLite MEDIUM x2 (password column, NoteDatabaseHelper INSERT and SQLInjection CREATE TABLE), 5 tapjacking; OVAA and InsecureShop broadcast hits identical to the old rule (1 and 2); NewPipe 8 broadcasts (7 LOW custom in-app actions sent implicitly = real but low; AudioReactor LOW after the platform-extra fix), 1 SQLite INFO, 0 tapjacking.
  - Tests: `IrRulesTest` 21 (register reuse, phi of explicit+implicit, phi with a parameter, escape to a helper, setPackage(null), null vs real permission, LBM, platform action, hashed password, PII through StringBuilder, constant-class column, SQLCipher, plugin OFF after ON in the same register, ON on one branch via valueOf); `IrRules0750Test` 8 (Kotlin wrapper, tapjacking, ApkFacts, `RuleRegistry.audit()` empty). Mutations of the permission guard, escape guard, hash guard and phi text each fail exactly one test.
  - Benchmark lists: PIVAA `tapjacking`, `plugin-state`, `sqlite-clear` got the new rule ids (`amended`). **Held-out set is now Allsafe only**: a new held-out app (Sieve has an APK; DIVA publishes none) is needed before a blind claim.
  - Not done: weak RNG; per-Activity tapjacking; inter-procedural PII; implicit `startActivity` hijack (a different weakness, CWE-927 too, already partly in implicit-intent-sensitive).
- 0.74.0 (previous):
  (kept below) 0.74.0 (unverified on device; `ir` 227/227 and Kotlin non-android tests 87/87 run in the sandbox (kotlinc 2.3.21 + android stubs + JUnit stub); `MainActivity.kt` / UI NOT compiled, not touched). Version 0.74.0 / code 170. **Fixes for the misses of the first held-out run** (see `tools/benchmark/HELDOUT_RESULTS.md`) plus the 3 audit gaps of 0.72.0.
  - **`tls-trustmanager` bug (the important one)**: `SecurityScanner.METHOD_PREAMBLE` skipped only dot-directive lines, so a method with `throws CertificateException` (an `.annotation system Ldalvik/annotation/Throws; value = { ... } .end annotation` block, whose inner lines do not start with a dot) was never matched: nearly every empty `checkServerTrusted`. The preamble now also skips whole `.annotation ... .end annotation` blocks (also inside `.param`). `METHOD_PREAMBLE` is used by exactly two rules (`tls-trustmanager`, `tls-hostname-verifier`); there is no other user. Real smali: PIVAA `API$2` is now CRITICAL (was missed); Vuldroid, Allsafe, NewPipe unchanged. Tests: `HeldOutFixes0740Test` (3 positives fail with the old regex, the negative twins pass).
  - **Constant `byte[]` (new-array + fill-array-data)** as IV / key: `SecurityScanner.constByteArrayArg` walks back at most 30 lines in the method, follows `move-object`, reads the `.array-data 1` block, and returns null if the register is touched by an aput / an invoke that gets the array (so a later `nextBytes(iv)` is never called constant). Used by `crypto-static-iv` (IvParameterSpec; new: `GCMParameterSpec(I[B)` nonce) and `crypto-hardcoded-key` (SecretKeySpec). The key value is never printed. PIVAA: 2 static IV + 3 hardcoded key (the PIVAA hardcoded-key miss had the same cause). `new-array` with NO fill-array-data (all zeros) is NOT judged.
  - **MD5/SHA-1 name passed to a hash helper** (`crypto-weak-hash`): class-local heuristic, a call whose method name says hash / digest / checksum / md5 with the constant algorithm as first (static) or second argument; MEDIUM confidence, the value is not followed into the helper. NOTE: this block was already in the working tree when I resumed after the first part of the session (not written by the author of this entry); it was reviewed, its 4 tests pass, and it was measured on the 4 apps (PIVAA 12 weak-hash hits incl. library DigestUtils, NewPipe 2 in androidx.work IdGenerator, both MEDIUM/LOW): library hits are lowered by `FindingPolish` as before.
  - **`ir` engine (`TaintIr`)**: (1) `Parcel.readString / createStringArray / readBundle / readParcelable / readSerializable ...` is a level-1 source inside `<init>` / `createFromParcel` / `readFromParcel` / `onTransact`, only on the method's own Parcel parameter (`viaParamOfType`, generalised from `viaIntentParam`); OVAA `MemoryCorruptionParcelable.<init>` -> `Class.forName` found (MEDIUM/LOW, reach=N because the framework calls the constructor, the graph cannot). (2) `ZipEntry.getName` (+ JarEntry, commons-compress entries) is a level-1 source (Zip Slip); the existing canonical-path / startsWith validation detection lowers the confidence; NO real app produced a Zip flow in the 6 apps measured, so this one is unit-tested only. (3) **Intent redirection** (`T_REDIRECT`, HIGH, CWE-940): `startActivity / startActivityForResult / startService / startForegroundService / sendBroadcast / bindService` whose Intent argument is the OBJECT from `Intent.getParcelableExtra()`. Matched by method name + signature (the owner is usually the app's own Activity class), kept OUT of `SINKS` on purpose (SINKS feeds the scope planner). Only that source counts (`REDIRECT_SOURCES`): an Intent that merely carries tainted extras (putExtra, putExtras(getExtras()), the parcelable wrapped as an extra of a fixed Intent) stays quiet (tests). Real: InsecureShop `WebView2Activity.onCreate`, Vuldroid `RoutingActivity.handleIntentExtras`, Allsafe `ProxyActivity.onCreate`: all 3 read in smali = textbook redirection. Limit: `getExtras().getParcelable(..)` is not covered.
  - **Measured (MeasureBig, old vs new `ir`)**: OVAA 4->5 (Parcel), InsecureShop 5->8 (1 redirection + 2 LOW/LOW `net/gotev/uploadservice` flows from Parcel in library-like code, reach=N, to be judged), Vuldroid 4->5 and Allsafe 4->5 (redirection), PIVAA 2 and NewPipe 1 unchanged.
  - `VulnKnowledge`: entries for CWE-940 and CWE-73 (the latter was used since 0.72.0 without an entry).
  - **Held-out rule applied**: PIVAA's fixes (TrustManager, IV, key, MD5 helper) were made from its misses, so **PIVAA moved to the tuning set**; Allsafe and Vuldroid remain held-out for the fixes above except that the redirection finds were not in their README lists (not recall misses). A NEW held-out app (Ghera, Sieve, ...) is needed before any new benchmark claim. First held-out numbers (frozen): 25/39 = 64%; 28/39 = 72% after 3 mistakes in the expected lists were corrected.
  - **Not done**: weak RNG (design decision, needs a security-context word next to `Random`), tapjacking / setPluginState / unencrypted SQLite / intent-sniffing rules, PIVAA "JS enabled" and "hardcoded data" (not diagnosed), `getExtras().getParcelable` redirection, Parcel source in grouped (big app) mode (the scope planner only marks classes by static sources), device run of the full scanner on the re-fixed apps.
  - **Owner to do**: build, then Smart scan PIVAA / Allsafe / Vuldroid and `bench.py ... --measure` again (these are now post-fix numbers, not independent); check the 2 `gotev` flows and the redirection cards on InsecureShop.

## Version 0.72.0 (previous)
- 0.72.0 (unverified on device; `ir` compiled and tested in the sandbox: 211/211; Kotlin `ScanRulesTaint.kt` compiled with kotlinc 2.3.21 together with every non-android `.kt`; `MainActivity.kt` NOT compiled). ## Version 0.71.0 (previous)
- 0.71.0 (unverified on device; `MainActivity.kt` NOT compiled here (needs the Android SDK); `ScanCompare.kt` compiled with kotlinc 2.3.21 and tested in the sandbox). Version 0.71.0 / code 167. **Before / After compare** (item 2 of the owner's list).
  - **What it does**: Security tab, new row under the filter bar: `Save baseline` stores a snapshot of the current findings in `filesDir/baselines/<package>.json` (outside the workspace, so importing another APK does not delete it); `Compare with baseline` diffs the current scan against it and shows a dialog (OK / Copy) with the Markdown result: NEW, WORSE, FIXED, BETTER, CHANGED, unchanged count, a verdict (IMPROVED / MIXED / REGRESSED / NO CHANGE) and the priority-score total before -> after. Both buttons refuse to run until a Deep scan (code) has been done, otherwise "fixed" would only mean "not scanned yet".
  - **Rules (`ScanCompare.kt`, pure Kotlin + org.json)**: INFO and library findings are left out (same as the report). A finding = ruleId + title + location (never the char offset `at`). The AI verdict is NOT used (unstable between runs, same reason as `FindingScore`). Same rule + same location + same CWE but a different title = CHANGED (not one fixed + one new). Duplicate keys merge (worst severity, summed count). A damaged / foreign JSON gives "no baseline", never a crash. The report always ends with the note that "fixed" = "no longer reported", not proof.
  - Tests: `ScanCompareTest` 16/16. Mutation (second-pass matching disabled) makes `sameRuleAndPlaceWithNewTitleIsChangedNotFixedPlusNew` fail. `ir` still 203/203.
  - Sandbox for Kotlin (new this session): kotlinc 2.3.21 from the JetBrains GitHub release + 4 tiny Java Android stubs (`Context`, `PackageManager`, `PermissionInfo`, `AssetManager`) + `libandroid-json-org-java` jar; compile every `.kt` with no `import android`/`androidx` (except `Rebuild.kt`) plus `PermissionGuard.kt` and the test, run with `tools/sandbox/junit-stub/RunAll`.
  - Not done: the diff is not exported as a file (dialog + Copy only); no per-finding "open in code" from the dialog; one baseline per package (a new Save replaces it).
  - Measured baseline of this session (0.70.0, 7 apps, `MeasureBig`): OVAA 3, InsecureBankv2 4, AndroGoat 3, InsecureShop 4, InjuredAndroid 4, Aegis 1 (MEDIUM/LOW), NewPipe 1 (HIGH/HIGH, `ReCaptchaActivity` WebView, NOT reviewed yet). The first five are identical to the numbers recorded in 0.68.4.

## Version 0.70.0 (previous)
- 0.70.0 (unverified on device; `ir` compiled and tested in the sandbox, no Kotlin/UI touched). Version 0.70.0 / code 166. **The Intent handed to `onActivityResult` is a weak source** (it was planned as "allocation-site heap"; measurement said that was the wrong lever, see below).
  - **Why not the heap**: the fields that carry real Intent taint in Nextcloud (the biggest FP source, 15 flows, 11 of them HIGH) are single-instance fields (`MainApp.storagePath`, `SettingsActivity.storagePath`, prefs `storage_path`), not shared object fields like `OCFile.remotePath`. Object-sensitivity cannot split a field that has one instance. The root cause is that `SettingsActivity.onActivityResult(requestCode, resultCode, data)` reads the folder-picker answer with `data.getStringExtra(...)` and the engine called it attacker input. `HeapKey` (0.69.0) stays as groundwork; build the allocation-site heap only when a real app shows a merged-object-field FP.
  - **Rule** (`TaintIr.Intra.invoke`): an `Intent` getter whose receiver is (a move / cast / phi of) the method's own `Intent` parameter inside a method named `onActivityResult` yields `Tv.internalSource` (weak + internal) with the source name suffixed `" in onActivityResult"` (`TaintIr.ACTIVITY_RESULT_SUFFIX`). It is a LEAD (LOW confidence), never removed, and a real source reaching the same sink still wins (the group shows the strong flow). `getIntent()` or a locally built Intent inside `onActivityResult`, and the Intent parameter of any other method, keep full strength. `SsaBridge` prints its own sentence ("the result that an activity this app started handed back ... attacker-controlled only if the app starts an implicit Intent that another app can answer") and skips the generic "An Intent is controlled by the caller" sentence for these.
  - **Measured (0.68.6 vs 0.70.0, 15 apps)**: Nextcloud 35.0.1: 7 HIGH/HIGH + 4 HIGH/MEDIUM + 3 MEDIUM/LOW + 1 LOW/LOW -> 14 MEDIUM/LOW + 1 LOW/LOW (same 15 flows, none dropped; no HIGH left). OVAA, InsecureBankv2, AndroGoat, InjuredAndroid, InsecureShop, DVBank, NewPipe, Aegis, NetGuard, AdAway, KeePassDX, Amaze, Mastodon, Home Assistant: flows and confidences IDENTICAL (all known benchmark TPs kept).
  - Tests: `IrActivityResultTest` 6 (weak in an exported activity, moved copy still weak, local Intent / getIntent-style stays strong, `onNewIntent` unaffected, real source on the same sink stays strong, report item LOW with its sentence). `ir` 203/203; with the rule switched off 4 positives fail, the 2 twins pass.
  - Not covered: the older Kotlin in-class taint engine (`ScanRulesTaint`) has no such rule; `onActivityResult` of an app that deliberately launches an implicit Intent another app can answer (intent-hijack) is now a LOW lead instead of HIGH. `tools/sandbox/MeasureBig` has `-Ddetail.max=<n>` for the full flow text.
- 0.69.3 (unverified on device; Kotlin compiled and the new tests run in the sandbox, MainActivity/UI not touched). Version 0.69.3 / code 165. **Per-finding priority score** (the "report scoring" idea from the Kimi table, the only part of it that was missing and cheap).
  - `FindingScore.of(f)` = `base(severity) x confidence x reachability x scope`, 1..100, bands P1 >= 70 / P2 >= 45 / P3 >= 25 / P4. base CRITICAL 90, HIGH 70, MEDIUM 45, LOW 20, INFO 5; confidence HIGH 1.0 / MEDIUM 0.85 / LOW 0.6; reach YES 1.0 / YES~ 0.9 / NO 0.7 / not traced 1.0; scope app 1.0 / obfuscated 0.85 / library 0.5. **It is an ordering estimate, not CVSS** (a static scan cannot know attack vector or impact). Deterministic; the AI verdict is deliberately NOT an input (not stable between runs). It changes no detection and no severity.
  - Shown as its own line `- Priority: 60/100 (P2)` in the Markdown report (own line on purpose: `tools/benchmark/bench.py` reads the Location / Rule / AI lines, so it is unaffected), in the HTML card, in the finding dialog (with the factors), and in the SARIF result as `rank` (SARIF 3.27.25, 0..100) + `properties.priorityBand/priorityWhy`.
  - Tests (sandbox): `FindingScoreTest` 9 (exact values, each factor only lowers, bands, library CRITICAL < app HIGH, AI verdict ignored, SARIF `rank`, HTML line) + `PermissionGuardTest` 6 = 15/15; `ir` still 197/197. Mutation (library factor 0.5 -> 1.0) makes 2 tests fail. INFO scores 3 because `FindingPolish.confidenceOf` is always LOW for INFO.
  - Why this and not `IntentFlow` first: none of the 15 apps measured in 0.69.1 contains a case where helper-class forwarding changes a result, so there is no evidence of benefit and it touches the taint engine (the `Intra` / `isInternalComponent` circularity noted in 0.69.1). Better next engine step for real false positives: 0.70.0 allocation-site heap (Nextcloud `OCFile.remotePath`, study cause 2).
  - Not done: the report is NOT re-sorted by score (the list order is unchanged); no score column in the Findings list UI.
- 0.69.2 (unverified on device; Kotlin of AiCache not compiled). Version 0.69.2 / code 164. **Review of another model's edits (Kimi k3, based on 0.69.0).** Merged: AiCache LRU (a hit calls `setLastModified`, 3 lines). NOT merged:
  - `SecurityScanner.applyPermissionContext` (lowers every finding in a signature-guarded exported component by one level). Run in the sandbox (`Finding` list + manifest): (a) a hardcoded key and weak crypto in a guarded service were lowered although the permission has nothing to do with them; (b) a CRITICAL taint finding in an activity that an UNGUARDED exported activity-alias also exposes was lowered (confused deputy; the 0.69.1 `PermissionGuard`/`CallGraph` rule keeps it strong); (c) a provider with `android:permission`=signature but `writePermission`=normal was lowered for a write sink. It also double-lowers SSA taint findings that 0.69.1 already marks LOW, runs AFTER `secSorted` (list order stale), duplicates the manifest parser (`ManifestContext` already has it), and never fires on Termux (`RUN_COMMAND` is `protectionLevel="dangerous"`; its suggested Termux test cannot show anything). If the Kotlin in-class taint engine (`ScanRulesTaint`) should also honour guards, do it with `PermissionGuard.guardFor` and the forwarder rule, only for findings that come from an Intent/provider-parameter source.
  - `Rebuild.kt` `40 -> 30`: no demonstrated effect (dexlib2 opcode set is the same for API 30 and 34) and the "writes version 040" claim could not be checked (the dexlib2 inside apktool.jar is stripped). The `else -> 34` default already covers 040.
  - His gap table (CSV) is partly wrong against the code: TrustManager/HostnameVerifier rules (`tls-trustmanager`, hostname), Static IV, weak hash/DES, SHA1PRNG, 6 WebView rules, external storage, sensitive logging, deep-link host validation, NSC pinning/cleartext, native `.so` version strings (ScanLibraries) and SARIF 2.1.0 (with `$schema`) all exist (91 rule objects). Still valid: OSV/NVD matching beyond the 28-CVE local table, native `.so` symbol/crypto spotting, a per-finding priority score, context sensitivity for Intent sources.
- 0.69.1 (unverified on device; Kotlin of MainActivity NOT compiled; everything else compiled and run in the sandbox, see below). Version 0.69.1 / code 163. **Signature-guarded exported components are no longer attacker seeds** (P2 of the "context model" plan, merged with the entry-point filter).
  - **What it does**: an exported component whose `android:permission` is signature-level stays an ENTRY of the call graph (reach labels, entry paths) but is not a seed of `attackerReach`. An Intent read in it is then "internal" = LOW with its own sentence ("protected by a signature-level permission ..."), never removed. **If an unguarded exported component forwards an Intent to it (or is an open redirector) it stays strong**: that forwarder runs with the app's own identity and walks through the permission check (confused deputy). A guarded component that forwards does NOT propagate (only same-key apps can drive it). This reuses the 0.68.6 machinery (`isInternalComponent`, forwarding closure, `openRedirector`); the first idea (lower the severity after the solver from the permission alone) was dropped because it would have hidden exactly that case.
  - Code: `CallGraph.setGuardedComponents/isGuardedComponent`, `SsaBridge.newFlows(..., guarded)` (+ sentence), `SsaGroups.Host.guardedEntries()` (default empty), `MainActivity.guardedEntryTypes()` (ManifestContext + `PermissionGuard`, any failure = no guard). Providers/receivers are still never "internal" (unchanged 0.68.6 rule), so their guards change nothing yet. A provider uses the weaker of read/write (`Access.ANY`).
  - 0.69.0 (same zip lineage): `TaintIr.HeapKey` (object space + field, only the GLOBAL space is used = no behaviour change; 0.70.0 puts an allocation site in `obj`), `ManifestComponent.permission/readPermission/writePermission/targetActivity`, `ManifestContext.packageName/smaliType/smaliTypeOf`, `PermissionGuard.kt` (NONE / WEAK / SIGNATURE; provider read/write independent; an exported activity-alias without a permission defeats a guarded target).
  - **Tests (sandbox, JUnit stub)**: `ir` 197/197 (185 old + 12 new `IrGuardedSourceTest`; 5 positives FAIL with the guard seeding switched off, the 7 twins pass either way). `PermissionGuardTest` 6/6 (Kotlin 2.3.21 compiled here with an android stub; a mutation "strongest wins" makes it fail). All Kotlin scanner files except Rebuild.kt and the UI compile with 0 errors.
  - **Measured (harness `MeasureBig` + `GuardList`, 0.68.6 vs 0.69.1)**: OVAA, InsecureBankv2, AndroGoat, InjuredAndroid, InsecureShop, DVBank, NewPipe 0.29.1, Aegis 3.4.3, NetGuard 2.337, AdAway 6.1.4, KeePassDX 4.5.5, Amaze 3.11.3, Mastodon 2.13.4, Home Assistant 2026.8.4, Nextcloud 35.0.1: **flows and confidences identical, time unchanged** (Home Assistant has 49 guarded entries, Nextcloud 2). So this version changes nothing on this corpus: the signature-guarded exports found in real apps are system-bound services (`BIND_*` tiles, VPN, autofill, widgets) that carry no Intent taint. It is a safety/precision rule for apps with their own signature permission; there is no real-app proof of benefit yet.
  - **Correction**: Termux `RUN_COMMAND` (the case that motivated this) is declared `dangerous`, not signature: `PermissionGuard` reports WEAK and Termux is NOT downgraded. A dangerous permission is not a guard (any app can request it); no "dangerous -> MEDIUM" tier was built.
  - Sandbox: `tools/sandbox/GuardList.java` (real Kotlin `ManifestContext` + `PermissionGuard` on a text manifest, writes the guarded types) and `MeasureBig -Dguard.file=<that file>`. Needs the Kotlin classes compiled with kotlinc (see README).
  - Not done / next: 0.69.3 `IntentFlow` (forwarding derived from the SSA data flow instead of the same-class heuristic; note `Intra` calls `isInternalComponent`, so that pass needs its own mode that never asks for the reach set), 0.70.0 allocation-site heap.
- 0.68.6 (unverified on device; Kotlin NOT touched; `ir` tests here: **185/185 pass** = 172 + 13 new in `IrInternalSourceTest`; the positives FAIL with the rule switched off). Version 0.68.6 / code 161. First fix from the real-app study ("cause 1", see `tools/sandbox/REAL_APPS_STUDY.md`): **an Intent read in an activity / service the manifest does not export is not attacker data, so the flow is shown as LOW (never removed)**.
  - **What**: `CallGraph.isInternalComponent(type)` (class or the class it is nested in): an ACTIVITY or SERVICE (framework / androidx base readable) that is not in the component list the graph was built with (= exported ones) and not in `attackerReach`. `attackerReach` = exported components + their app superclasses + every component that an exported (or already reached) class launches with an explicit Intent (the existing "icc" edges: const-class + Intent builder) AND that reads Intent data itself, repeated to a fixpoint. If any reached class takes `getParcelableExtra/getParcelable` and calls `startActivity*/startService/sendBroadcast/bindService` in the same method (intent redirection pattern), `openRedirector` is set and NOTHING is internal. `TaintIr` makes such a source `Tv.internalSource` (weak + `internal`), `Flow.internal`, `SsaBridge.toItem` shows LOW and its own sentence ("... does not export ...").
  - **Why it is this conservative (measured, do not loosen without the benchmark)**: the first version (any unexported activity / service / receiver) lowered REAL vulnerabilities of the deliberate benchmarks: OVAA `WebViewActivity` (reached by a deeplink activity), InsecureShop `PrivateActivity` (intent redirection), InjuredAndroid `TestBroadcastReceiver$b` (a dynamic receiver inside an exported activity; dynamic receivers are exported by default). The engine counts every Intent getter as a source on purpose (see the 0.68.1 note in `TaintIr`: "the taint handed to an Activity launch ... every getStringExtra on the other side is a level-2 source"), so the rule only drops the flows where nothing launches the target. Receivers and providers are never internal.
  - **Measured (sandbox, harness with exported-only entries, 0.68.5 vs 0.68.6)**: OVAA, InsecureBankv2, AndroGoat, InjuredAndroid, InsecureShop, DVBank: flows and confidences **identical**. 11 real apps with flows: Aegis 1 -> 0 and Nextcloud 13 -> 11 "not LOW" cards (all three hand-verified false positives: `ImportEntriesActivity`, `ExternalSiteWebView`, `ErrorsWhileCopyingHandlerActivity$MoveFilesTask`); every other app unchanged; no flow removed anywhere. Total not-LOW 43 -> 40 (my estimate before measuring was 15-20: wrong, the guard keeps most flows strong). Not measured: which guard (forwarding closure or redirect pattern) held each app.
  - **Not covered (still false positives)**: an Intent read in a helper class (Focus `CrashReporter`), a dynamic receiver for protected system broadcasts (Mihon: needs the registered action), Termux `ReportActivity` (launched by an exported component that reads Intent data). Ideas: follow the Intent value across the launch (put/get extras) instead of the class-level proxy; protected-broadcast list for dynamic receivers.
  - **Next, ordered**: (b) `android:permission` of the entry component into severity and detail (Termux RUN_COMMAND); (c) shared data-class field (per-object heap, Nextcloud `OCFile.remotePath`); guard recognition (Amaze flag + `isValidPath`).

## Version 0.68.5 (previous)
- 0.68.5 (unverified on device; Kotlin NOT touched; `ir` tests run here (javac 21 via the JRE `jdk.compiler` module, JUnit stub): **172/172 pass** = 165 old + 7 new in `IrWeakTaintTest`; the 3 positives FAIL with the marking switched off, the other 4 are clean twins / the join unit test). Version 0.68.5 / code 160. **Owner decision (the "Open" item of 0.68.4): mark the taint that passed an Object field of an obfuscated class as weak, shown as LOW confidence.**
  - **What** (`TaintIr` + `SsaBridge`, **changes confidence only, never removes a flow**): `Tv.weak` (new). `FIELD_GET` of a field whose type is `Ljava/lang/Object;` in a class of the DEFAULT package (R8 name such as `aq3`; helper `Intra.isMergedObjectField`) turns a real taint into a weak one. Join rule (`Tv.rank()`): a STRONG source of any level beats a weak one (AND-join), inside the same strength the higher level wins; `covers` uses the same rank. The mark travels through `raiseField` (fields keep it), the return summary (`Summary.retWeak`, also in the fingerprint) and `Flow.weak`. Dedupe (`addFlow`, `best` in `analyze`) never replaces a strong flow by a weak one. `SsaBridge.toItem`: a weak flow gets confidence LOW (so severity one level lower, like the prefs case) and the detail gets one sentence ("... can be a false positive (low confidence); check the chain ..."); when a group of flows of one key holds a strong one, that one is shown.
  - **Not weak on purpose**: a `String`-typed field of the same class, an `Object` field of a packaged class (`t/B`), anything that is not a field read (the prefs heap, calls, arrays).
  - **Measured in the sandbox, 0.68.4 vs 0.68.5 (same harness, `MeasureBig`, now prints `[sev/confidence]`)**: **Bitwarden 2026.9.1: same 20 flows, confidence 12 HIGH + 7 MEDIUM + 1 LOW -> 20 LOW** (groups time 113 s -> 108 s, 1 thread). OVAA 3, InsecureBankv2 4 (1 LOW as before), AndroGoat 3, InjuredAndroid 4, InsecureShop 4, DVBank 1, Mastodon 2.13.4 3 (1 LOW), Wikipedia 5 (4 LOW), NewPipe 1: **flows AND confidences identical** (the flow lines were diffed). K-9 and Element X were not re-run (0 flows in 0.68.4; the change cannot add a flow).
  - **Known limit (honest)**: a REAL flow that goes through an Object field of an obfuscated class (for example a generic holder in an R8 app) is also shown as LOW now. It stays in the list and its chain is still there; the trade-off was accepted by the owner for the big R8 apps. If a real case shows up, narrow `isMergedObjectField` (for example only classes with an `iget-byte` + `packed-switch` discriminator, see `tools/sandbox/mergedscan.py`).
  - **Checks on device (optional)**: JustMarkets `Deep scan status` + cards: the OBF cards whose chain goes through a field of an obfuscated class should be LOW now; OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11 and AndroGoat `no extra flow` unchanged.
  - **Real-app study (sandbox, same session; details in `tools/sandbox/REAL_APPS_STUDY.md`)**: 15 open-source apps, 59 flows, 43 not LOW; 14 reviewed by hand = 0 vulnerabilities (all false positives or by-design). Causes: (1) an Intent read in a NOT exported component / app-built result Intent / system broadcast is still a level-2 source, (2) taint kept per field so one write taints every read (Nextcloud `OCFile.remotePath`), (3) project-local guards not understood (Amaze flag + `isValidPath`), (4) `android:permission` of the entry component ignored (Termux `RUN_COMMAND`). **The harness `MeasureBig` now uses exported components only (as the app does); the old harness treated every component as an entry, so older `reach=Y` numbers in this file were optimistic.**
  - **Next (not done), ordered**: (a) a source counts as attacker data only when the component that reads it is exported; (b) entry permission into severity and detail; (c) shared data-class field (per-object heap); then the older items: look at the plausible flows (`SingleWebViewActivity` in Wikipedia, `TweakedFileProvider` in Mastodon) with the owner; method-level R8 rescue only if a measured lost real flow shows up; parameter labels into fields.

## Version 0.68.4 (previous)
- 0.68.4 (unverified on device; Kotlin NOT touched; `ir` tests run here (javac 21 via the JRE `jdk.compiler` module, JUnit stub): **165/165 pass** = 155 old + 10 new in `IrModels0684Test`; the 6 positives FAIL with the 0.68.3 `TaintIr`, the 4 clean twins are negative controls). Version 0.68.4 / code 159. **Measured in the sandbox on 11 real APKs with the real library filter + `ScopePlanner.groups` + `SsaGroups.run`** (`tools/sandbox/MeasureBig.java`; no device, no JustMarkets needed): OVAA, InsecureBankv2, AndroGoat, InjuredAndroid, InsecureShop, DVBank, NewPipe 0.29.1, Mastodon 2.13.3, K-9 Mail 24.0, Wikipedia (alpha, latest), Element X 26.09.4; Bitwarden 2026.9.1 only as a baseline (see "Open" below).
  - **What** (`TaintIr` tables only, **changes results**): `BUILDERS` += `kotlin/Pair`, `kotlin/Triple`, `android/database/MatrixCursor`, `org/apache/http/message/BasicNameValuePair`, `okhttp3/Headers$Builder`, `HttpUrl$Builder`, `FormBody$Builder`, `Request$Builder`. `PURE` += `kotlinx/serialization/json/Json` (+ `$Default`), `android/util/Base64`, `java/util/Base64` (+ `$Decoder`, `$Encoder`), `java/util/regex/Pattern`, `Matcher`. `kindOf`: every `java/time/` and `j$/time/` class is pure (`LocalDate.parse(text)`). Chosen from the real drops that repeated or were largest: `kotlin/Pair.<init>` 9 drops in 3 apps, `Json.decodeFromString` 35 (Wikipedia), `Base64.decode` (Bitwarden), `LocalDate.parse` 9, `Headers$Builder.add` 4, `Pattern.matcher` 2, `MatrixCursor.addRow` 3. NOT modelled on purpose: `Locale$Builder` (not security relevant), `Preferences$Key` / `MutablePreferences` (needs a heap model), obfuscated library classes (`kee`, `xea`, `t85` ... no stable name).
  - **Measured effect (grouped harness, before -> after flows)**: OVAA 3->3, InsecureBankv2 3->**4**, AndroGoat 3->3, InjuredAndroid 4->4, InsecureShop 4->4, DVBank 1->1, NewPipe 1->1, Mastodon 3->3, K-9 0->0, Wikipedia 5->5, Element 0->0. The one new flow is REAL: InsecureBankv2 `DoTransfer$RequestDoTransferTask$1.run` takes the username (SharedPreferences, through `Base64.decode`, field `DoTransfer.usernameBase64ByteString`) and builds the `Statements_<user>.html` path for `FileWriter` on external storage (checked in the smali, 2 places). `library real` drops: InsecureBankv2 22->20, InsecureShop 3->1, NewPipe 30->29, K-9 36->32; Wikipedia 123->**134** (more taint stays alive, so more of it reaches an unmodelled call later: Context 10, Fragment 10, Service 10 ... not a regression). Times unchanged.
  - **Checks on device (optional now)**: JustMarkets `Deep scan status` (`library real` same or lower, new cards judged by `via` + chain). OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11 (+ the new file-path card is expected), AndroGoat `no extra flow`.
  - **Sandbox experiment on the R8 scope idea (done, NOT built)**: keep a LIB-scoped class that mentions types of the app's own package (`-Drescue=true`). Result: OVAA / InsecureBankv2 / AndroGoat / InjuredAndroid 0 rescued, flows identical; InsecureShop 1 and DVBank 2 rescued, flows identical; NewPipe 218 rescued, flows 1->3 but BOTH new flows are inside ACRA library code (`org/acra/builder/ReportBuilder.build` ... in R8-merged `JsonBuilder` / `Cookie$Companion`, source `Intent.getData()` through a merged `PopupMenu$1.toString()`) = false positives; NewPipe time taint 1.5->3.3 s, `library real` 30->77. A merged class mixes library and app methods, so keeping the whole class brings the library in. A naive version (any type, not the app package) pulls Material / androidx into the scope on obfuscated apps (InjuredAndroid, DVBank). A METHOD-level rescue would be the precise version; nothing measured shows a lost real flow, so it is not planned.
  - **Open, measured, NOT changed (needs an owner decision because it changes what a severity means)**: precision on big R8 apps. Bitwarden (21k classes, 26 groups, 136 s with 2 threads) gives 20 flows and almost all look false: `Intent.getExtras()` through an obfuscated iterator `aq3.next()` (R8 merged several Kotlin sequence iterators into one class: `byte H` discriminator, `packed-switch`, ONE shared `Q:Object` field) or `o3a.getValue()` (Compose state holder) into `File.delete()`, `FileInputStream`, `Class.forName`. Cause: taint is stored per FIELD (`Class->name:type`), so one tainted write anywhere taints every read of an `Object`-typed field of a merged class. Checked and NOT the cause: many writers per field (only 1 of 257 tainted fields had 10 or more writers); `packed-switch` alone (only 5 of 16 chain methods are switch methods). Ideas: (a) a `weak` mark on a taint that passed an `Object`-typed field of an obfuscated (default-package) class, kept in `Tv` (an AND-join: a strong path wins), shown as LOW confidence; (b) object-sensitivity for merged classes (big). In the app these classes are OBF scope so the Kotlin side already lowers them, but they still fill the list. Wikipedia (5 flows, 1 plausible: `SingleWebViewActivity` loads the Intent url into a WebView) and Mastodon (3, one plausible: `TweakedFileProvider`) look much cleaner.
  - Tools: `tools/sandbox/` (README has the exact steps; `MeasureBig` = the app pipeline on big apps, `Measure` = one group, `gen_scope.py` regenerates the library filter from `SecurityScanner.kt`, `diag_patch.py` makes the diagnostic `TaintIr` with the method name in the drop key).

## Version 0.68.3 (previous)
- **Sandbox experiment after 0.68.3 (NO app code changed, version stays 0.68.3; new folder `tools/sandbox/` = the harness, see its README).** Question: should the Deep scan keep a LIB-scoped class that mentions types of the app's own package (R8 merged app code into library-named classes)? Tried as `-Drescue=true` in the harness, with the real `libPrefixes` filter, on the 7 apps. **Result: NOT worth building.** OVAA / InsecureBankv2 / AndroGoat / InjuredAndroid: 0 classes rescued, flows identical; InsecureShop 1 and DVBank 2 classes rescued, flows identical; NewPipe 0.29.1: 218 classes rescued, flows 1 -> 3, but BOTH new flows are inside ACRA library code (`org/acra/builder/ReportBuilder.build` -> `File.renameTo` / `FileOutputStream` in R8-merged classes `JsonBuilder` / `Cookie$Companion`, source `Intent.getData()` through a merged `PopupMenu$1.toString()`), reach=Y only because of the merged names = false positives; no real app flow was gained. Cost: NewPipe parse 1.4 -> 2.3 s, graph 1.0 -> 1.8 s, taint 1.5 -> 3.3 s, `library real` drops 30 -> 77. Reason: a merged class mixes library methods and app methods, so keeping the whole class brings the library code in. A method-level rescue (only methods that mention app types) would be the precise version, but nothing measured here shows a lost real flow, so it is NOT planned unless JustMarkets numbers show one. A naive rule (any type, not the app package) is worse: on obfuscated apps (InjuredAndroid, DVBank) the obfuscated library classes `a/g/k/s` count as app code and hundreds of Material / androidx classes get pulled in.
- 0.68.3 (unverified on device; Kotlin NOT touched; Java part + all `ir` tests run here (javac 21 via the JRE `jdk.compiler` module, JUnit stub): **155/155 pass** = 146 old + 9 new in `IrCollectionsArraycopyTest`; 5 of the 9 FAIL with the 0.68.2 `TaintIr`, the other 4 are negative controls). Version 0.68.3 / code 158. **First version measured IN THE SANDBOX on real APKs** (the owner asked Claude to download the open-source apps and test by itself): OVAA, InsecureBankv2, AndroGoat, InjuredAndroid, InsecureShop, Damn Vulnerable Bank (GitHub releases) and NewPipe 0.29.1, each decoded with apktool 2.10.0 and run through `CallGraph` + `TaintIr.analyze(g, false, null)` by a small harness (not in the zip; the Kotlin scanner, Smart scan and the benchmark are NOT run here). **Result 0.68.2 vs 0.68.3: the flows are IDENTICAL on all 7 apps** (3 / 3 / 3 / 4 / 6 / 1 / 14 distinct flow lines), time unchanged (NewPipe 11 s). So this version found no new flow on them; it closes losses that were visible in the NewPipe numbers. NewPipe `library or outside group real` 742 -> 392.
  - **Where the NewPipe real drops were** (diagnostic build with the method name in the owner key): `AbstractCollection.size / iterator / isEmpty` 68, `TextView.setText` 28, the D8 string helpers `Owner$-CC.m / mm` and `Modifier$-CC.mm` (`StringBuilder` concatenation, `String ... -> String`) about 100, `System.arraycopy` 16, plus many primitive results.
  - `BUILDERS` (**changes results**): `AbstractCollection / AbstractList / AbstractSet / AbstractMap / AbstractQueue / AbstractSequentialList`, `LinkedHashSet`, `TreeSet`, `SortedSet / NavigableSet / SortedMap / NavigableMap`, `Vector`, `Stack`, `Hashtable`, `WeakHashMap`, `IdentityHashMap`, `EnumMap`, `PriorityQueue`, the `java/util/concurrent` maps / lists / queues, `ArrayMap`, `ArraySet` (platform and androidx), `SparseArray`, `ContentValues` are containers like `ArrayList` (before: unknown library = taint dropped).
  - `TaintIr.Intra.invoke` (**changes results**): `System.arraycopy(src, ., dest, ., .)` adds the taint of `src` to `dest` (`raiseObj`); a static helper in a class named `...$-CC` (D8 desugaring companion, never a framework API) that returns `String` and takes only `String` / `CharSequence` / primitives hands the join of its arguments to the result.
  - `noLossDrop` (counter only): a primitive result (`ZIJFDSBC`) and a void call on a UI toolkit class (`android/widget`, `android/view`, `androidx/appcompat`, `androidx/recyclerview`, material, `compose/runtime`) are not counted as a lost taint.
  - **Still open, seen on NewPipe, NOT changed**: R8 merged real app code into classes named like libraries (`kotlin/io/CloseableKt` holds NewPipe extractor methods such as `get(String, Map, Localization)`, `androidx/sqlite/SQLite`, `androidx/work/OperationKt`). Because the library filter works by package prefix, that app code is not in the IR and the taint is dropped at those calls. A scope fix would need a different rule than the prefix (for example: a library class whose methods mention app types). Decide with the JustMarkets numbers.
  - **Checks on device**: the same as 0.68.2 (JustMarkets `Deep scan status`: `library real` lower, `+N flow(s)` same or higher, send any new card, groups time about 120 s, send `~ sites N`; OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11, AndroGoat `no extra flow`). New flows from the extra containers / `arraycopy` / `$-CC` helpers are possible on big obfuscated apps: judge each new card by its `via` and chain.

## Version 0.67.0 (previous)
- 0.67.0 (unverified on device; Kotlin NOT touched, so no new compile risk; Java part + all `ir` tests compiled and run here with javac 21 (JRE `jdk.compiler` module, JUnit stub): **135/135 pass** = 128 old + 7 new in `IrPrefsHeapTest`; 5 of the 7 FAIL with the old `TaintIr`, the other 2 are negative controls). Version 0.67.0 / code 153. **RESULTS CHANGE (first heap-model step), so it needs a measurement on device.**
  - **What**: `SharedPreferences$Editor.putString / putStringSet(key, value)`: a REAL source in `value` (Intent extra, prefs, provider parameter) is stored in the engine's field map under `prefs:<key literal>` and also under `prefs:?` (aggregate). A write whose key is not a `const-string` (checked through moves, `Intra.constString`) goes to `prefs:*`. `SharedPreferences.getString / getStringSet(key, def)`: result = the old prefs source (level 1) JOINED with `prefs:<key>` + `prefs:*`; a key that is not a literal, and `getAll()`, read `prefs:?`. So an Intent extra stored then read back keeps its Intent source (level 2, `via` = `field prefs:"k" (written in X)`) instead of becoming a plain `SharedPreferences.getString()` source (level 1). Readers are registered in `fieldReaders`, so a later write re-queues the reading methods like any field.
  - **Counter**: a put whose value carries a real source is consumed (not a `library` drop any more; the 25 `SharedPreferences$Editor` drops on JustMarkets should go). A put with a parameter-only value is still counted.
  - **Expected effect**: NEW flows are possible (Intent -> prefs -> sink in another method used to start from a level-1 prefs source: now level 2, higher severity / different `source` text, so a flow may change source and de-dup key `sink|source`). Nothing is removed.
  - **Known limits (honest)**: the prefs FILE name is ignored (same key in two files is mixed); keys built at run time are wildcards (more imprecise, never lossy); only String / StringSet values (the only prefs sources); heap is per group like the fields (a write and a read in different groups are not connected); parameter labels never go into the heap.
  - **Checks on device**: (1) OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11, AndroGoat 29/30 unchanged. (2) JustMarkets and Exness: the `Deep scan status` line: `library or outside group real` should drop by about 25 and `SharedPreferences$Editor` leaves the top owners; `+N flow(s)` may grow, send any new `taint-flow` card (look at `source` and `via`). (3) time: `plan ... + groups ...` should not move (was 122 s groups).
  - **Next (not done)**: the same for `Intent.putExtra` -> `getStringExtra` on one Intent object (object-based, needs aliasing), `Bundle`, then the `virtual~` / `interface~` edge kinds that `INVOKE_KINDS` ignores.

## Version 0.66.7 (previous)
- 0.66.7 (unverified on device; Java part + all `ir` tests compiled and run here with javac 21: **123/123 pass** = 117 old + 6 new in `IrTaintOnlyEdgesTest`, JUnit stub; no Kotlin change). Version 0.66.7 / code 150. **First change of RESULTS since 0.66.0** (0.66.5 / 0.66.6 only measured). **0.66.6 result (owner, 5 small apps)**: no regression (OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11, InjuredAndroid +1 `TestBroadcastReceiver$b`, InsecureShop +1 `PrivateActivity`, DamnVulnerableBank 1 WebView flow), caps all 0, slowest method 25 ms. Gap: InsecureBankv2 `provider-update` taint-flow not shown. **Finding**: `CallGraph` only visits methods an entry reaches, so a method no entry reaches has NO out-edges; a call from it into an app method that has a sink got no callee edge and the taint was dropped (small experiment: reachable caller flow=1, unreachable caller flow=0, counted as `app method unresolved` real=1). This explains InsecureShop's 67 real drops at `UploadTaskParameters` and is the suspected cause of `slice=0` on Exness (not proven on the big apps).
  - **Fix 1 (`CallGraph`)**: after the normal build, `addTaintOnlyEdges()` gives every app method with a body that is NOT reachable edges for the taint engine only: `static` / `direct` exact, `super` by dispatch, `virtual` / `interface` by CHA (every concrete app class below the owner, at most `TAINT_CHA_MAX` = 64 targets per site, sites over it counted in `taintChaCapped`; owner not an app class = one external edge). Stored apart in `taintOut` / `taintIn`; read through `taintEdgesFrom(key)` (own edges, or the taint-only ones for an unreachable method) and `taintCallersOf(key)`. **Nothing else changed**: `reach()` is not called, `out` / `in` / `reachable` / `externalCalls` / `pathTo` / `callersOf` / `edgesFrom` are as before, a flow whose origin is not reachable still says `reach NO`. `stats()` appends `N taint-only edges from unreachable methods` only when N > 0. Not done on purpose: clinit / callback / icc edges for unreachable methods.
  - `TaintIr`: `Intra` reads callees through `taintEdgesFrom`, the requeue after a summary change uses `taintCallersOf`. With `reachableOnly = true` the scope is only reachable methods, so nothing changes there.
  - **Fix 2 (`TaintIr`, counter only)**: a call that IS a sink (`SINKS` has `owner->name`) is no longer counted as a library / unresolved drop (`android/webkit/WebView`, `SQLiteDatabase` were the top owners but the flow was already reported there). Results unchanged.
  - Tests: `IrTaintOnlyEdgesTest` (static chain from unreachable code gives the flow with `reach NO`; interface call resolves to the implementation with the sink; reachability and edge queries unchanged; reachable caller adds no taint-only edge; sink call not a drop; non-sink library call still counted). Checked that 4 of the 6 FAIL with the two fixes switched off (flow 0 instead of 1, drop 1 instead of 0).
  - **Not fixed, same family (decide from the numbers)**: reachable call sites that RTA left unlinked and the CHA fallback linked get kind `virtual~` / `interface~`; `TaintIr.INVOKE_KINDS` skips those, so taint is dropped there too. Not changed because it widens results beyond this step.
  - **Checks on device** (same 5 apps, Smart scan): (1) `InsecureBankv2`: does `provider-update` appear as a taint-flow? (2) `InsecureShop`: `app method unresolved real` for `UploadTaskParameters` goes down; (3) OVAA 19/19, InsecureBankv2 11/11, AndroGoat 29/30 unchanged, new flows (with `reach NO`) are expected; (4) JustMarkets and Exness: status line `taint losses ...` (is `app method unresolved real` lower? does `+3 flows` grow?) and time (`plan ... + groups ...`); the CHA edges can make the groups slower; send the `Deep scan status` line.

## Version 0.66.5 (previous)
- 0.66.5 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; the whole `ir` package + all `ir` tests compiled with javac 21 and run here: **117/117 pass** = 111 old + 6 new in `IrStatsTest`, JUnit stub). Version 0.66.5 / code 148. **Goal: MEASUREMENT ONLY. No analysis result changes** (all 111 old tests unchanged and passing; the new code only counts). Reason: before touching the taint engine (heap model, summaries, speed) we need to know where it loses taint and what costs the time (Exness SSA: about 160 s CPU per group, Bitwarden about 3 s).
  - `TaintIr.Stats` (new, public, thread-safe `merge`, one-line `line()`), `TaintIr.Result.stats`, `SsaGroups.Result.stats` (summed over all groups; a group's stats are added only when the group finished), `SsaBridge.newFlows(..., Stats statsOut)` overloads (old signatures unchanged).
  - Counted, per method in its LAST analysis (a re-run replaces the count), as `real/param-only` (real = a real source was in the dropped argument, param-only = only a parameter label): **callee not analysed** (call edge to an app method with no summary: outside the scope, native/abstract), **app method unresolved** (call into a class the graph has, but no app callee edge), **library or outside group** (call the tables do not know: `kindOf == 0`, which includes an app class that is not in this group; the top 5 owners are listed, an `app ` prefix = a class of the graph).
  - Caps hit: **sink refs** (`MAX_REFS` = 16 per slot, number of refs dropped), **rounds** (methods whose last analysis still changed after `MAX_ROUNDS` = 30), **requeue** (methods that reached `MAX_REQUEUE` = 40).
  - **Slowest 5 methods** (total time over all analyses of the method, summed over groups, with instructions / most rounds one run needed / number of runs), measured with `System.nanoTime()` around `Run.analyse`.
  - Status: `MainActivity.ssaFindings` appends `gres.stats.line()` to `ssaScopeNote` (so it shows in the "Deep scan status" line of the report), right after the `time: plan ... cpu: ...` part. Format: `taint losses real/param-only: callee not analysed R/P, app method unresolved R/P, library or outside group R/P (top: owner n, ...); caps hit: sink refs N, rounds M method(s), requeue K method(s); slowest methods: Cls.m 12.3s [4521 insns, 7 rounds, 3 runs], ...`.
  - Not changed on purpose: `SsaForm`, `CallGraph`, `ScopePlanner`, the group planner, every Kotlin rule.
  - **Suspect if it fails to compile**: only the one added Kotlin line in `MainActivity.ssaFindings` (`if (gres.stats.analyses > 0) ssaScopeNote += ", " + gres.stats.line()`); the Java part is compiled.
  - **Checks on device**: (1) a small app (AndroGoat): the report has the new `taint losses ...` part and findings are the same as 0.66.4 (29/30, same taint flows). (2) Exness: read `slowest methods` (is one method over about 100 s? how many insns / rounds?) and `caps hit` (rounds > 0 means the 30-round sweep is the cost). (3) JustMarkets: same flows (+3); send the `callee not analysed` / `library` numbers and the top owners.
  - **Reading guide for the next step (not done, decide from the numbers)**: big `callee not analysed real` = the group caps lose real flows (scope problem, not engine). Big `library real`: owners with an `app ` prefix or an app package = out-of-group classes; `android/...` or `com/google/...` = missing tables (BUILDERS lacks ContentValues, LinkedHashSet, TreeSet, Vector, ConcurrentHashMap, ArrayMap, SparseArray). `rounds` > 0 or one slow method with thousands of insns = add a per-method round / instruction cap with a counter, or a def-use worklist instead of full sweeps. `sink refs` > 0 = raise MAX_REFS or keep the closest chains.
  - Engine gaps found while reading TaintIr (NOT fixed; there is no heap model: fields are field-based and real-source only): a callee does not mutate its arguments (`fill(bundle, x)`), parameter labels never go into fields, the sanitizer list is not per sink type, unknown library calls drop the taint, an app callee outside the scope returns no taint.

## Version 0.66.2 (previous)
- 0.66.2 (unverified, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; Java part compiled with javac 21, 111/111 IR tests pass locally). Version 0.66.2 / code 145. **0.66.1 result (owner, screenshots, com.exness.android.pa 42.0 MB, 4362 files)**: plan line `149 groups, 9 capped, biggest closure 3675 (callees=499 callers=31 callers2=395 supers=250 callees2=2499 slice=0 supers2=0)`; progress `group 3/149 - 2m53s elapsed, about 2h20m left` (groups 1-2 finished in 2 s, group 3 took ~2m50: very uneven). The report the owner exported mid-scan has only manifest / resource findings (12), no code findings yet. **Reading**: capping is NOT the problem here (9/149 = 6%); the cost is the NUMBER of groups. Even small batches reach the 3000 cap because `callees2` (2nd-level callees) is 2499 of 3675, so `groups()` halves the batch until it fits: 149 groups, each parsing / analysing up to ~3000 classes (the 0.60.0 soft cache helps only while the memory lasts). `slice=0` = the biggest group has no entry path at all (class-level graph finds no entry: Compose / DI / reflection) - reach will be NO there. Pruning `callees2` to marked classes would NOT be lossless: `TaintIr` drops taint at a callee that is not in the scope (`kind == 0` -> return), so an unmarked helper chain between source and sink must stay in scope.
  - **Time limit** (new, Settings button `SSA time limit: 5 / 10 / 20 / none`, default 10 min, pref `ssa_budget_min`): the group loop of a big app stops at the limit (`Host.cancelled()` also true when `ssaTimeUp`), keeps the flows found so far; status: `stopped at the 10 min time limit after N of M groups (P%)` and `INCOMPLETE: time limit: only P% of the groups were analysed (best classes first)`. Groups are planned best first (source+sink classes, then sources, then sinks), so the first groups are the most valuable. Stop scan now also adds `INCOMPLETE: stopped: only P% ...`. JustMarkets (about 2 min) is unaffected.
  - **Measurement**: `SsaGroups.Result.parseMs` / `analyseMs` (summed over threads) and the status ends `cpu: parse Ns + analysis Ns (summed over threads)`: tells whether the time is reading / parsing classes or the call graph + SSA + taint, which decides the next fix (shared summaries across groups = method-level plan, or larger groups if parsing dominates).
  - NOT done: raising the group cap, sharing summaries across groups, a reachable-only mode (lossy, off).
  - Owner: rebuild, run on Exness with the default limit (10 min), send the report (status line with coverage and `cpu:` split). Also run JustMarkets once to confirm no change.

## Version 0.65.0 (previous)
- 0.65.0 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; Java part compiled with javac 21, 110/110 IR tests pass locally: 107 old + 3 new in `IrGroupsTest`, run with a JUnit stub). Version 0.65.0 / code 142. **0.64.0 result (owner, JustMarkets)**: `301 groups, 320 marked of 26572, 212 capped, 994008 cut, biggest closure 8839 (callees=2 callers=2448 callers2=1012 supers=308 callees2=0 slice=4536 supers2=532), 506 hubs, plan 40s + groups 1613s on 2 threads`, `no extra flow`; report HIGH 3 / MEDIUM 21 / LOW 53 (77 listed of 268). **Reading of the numbers**: `callees=2` = the worst batch is ONE class (halving reached size 1); `callers=2448` = that single MARKED class has 2448 direct callers (a prefs / logging / util wrapper; marked classes were never hubs, so all its callers were pulled in); `slice=4536` = class-level CHA makes the entry slice huge. 301 groups for 320 marked classes = about one group per class, each re-parsing up to 3000 classes. The 0.64.0 per-tier shares only redistribute what is cut.
  - **Step 1 (honesty)**: path bar / report `Deep scan status` no longer says a bare `no extra flow` when groups were capped: `SSA taint: no extra flow in the analysed scope - INCOMPLETE: 212 of 301 groups capped (70%), flows outside the cut scope are not checked (...)`. Same `INCOMPLETE` text after `+N flow(s)`. New field `MainActivity.ssaIncomplete`.
  - **Step 2 (marked hubs)**: `ScopePlanner.groups` computes `markedHub` = marked class (source and/or sink) with >= `HUB_CALLERS` (60) callers. In `closure`: the callers tier skips batch members that are marked hubs; callers-of-callers, callees-of-callees and the backward slice use `hubAll` = hubs + marked hubs (a marked hub reached as a neighbour joins but is not expanded); the slice starts from the batch WITHOUT marked hubs, and each marked hub keeps ONE shortest entry path (`entryPath`, BFS over callers) so reach stays meaningful. `Groups.markedHubs`; path bar adds `, N marked hub(s): callers not expanded, one entry path kept`.
  - **Why this is nearly lossless**: a caller that contains a sink is itself marked, so it has its own group where the hub is a direct callee (test `aSinkCallerOfAMarkedHubStillSeesTheHubInItsOwnGroup`). **Known limit (honest)**: a flow hub -> unmarked intermediate class (no sink) -> sink class 2+ hops away is no longer in the hub's group (it still is, up to the cap, in the sink class's group only if the chain is within its 2 hops).
  - Tests: `aMarkedHubDoesNotPullItsCallersIntoItsOwnGroup`, `aSinkCallerOfAMarkedHubStillSeesTheHubInItsOwnGroup`, `aMarkedHubKeepsOneEntryPath`.
  - NOT done: the method-level skeleton + bottom-up summaries computed once for the whole app (the real fix for reparsing and the over-connected class graph): planned as 0.66.0+.
  - Owner: rebuild, Deep scan on JustMarkets, send the path bar (groups, capped, cut, biggest closure with tiers, marked hubs, time). Expected: far fewer groups and a much lower `callers` tier; if capped is still high the next target is `slice` (class-level CHA).

## Version 0.60.0 (previous)
- 0.60.0 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; Java part compiled with javac 21, 105/105 IR tests pass locally: 100 old + 5 new `IrSsaGroupsTest`). Version 0.60.0 / code 135. **Owner asked: faster SSA step WITHOUT lowering scan quality.** Nothing about the analysis changed (same groups, same call graph per group, same TaintIr, same merge); checked: on a synthetic 6000-class app with 20 overlapping groups the output of the new code is byte-identical to 0.59.3 for 1, 2 and 4 threads.
  - New `ir/SsaGroups.java` (Kotlin `ssaFindings` now just calls `SsaGroups.run(groups, entries, old, host, 200, threads)`): (1) a class is read (gunzip of the index file) and parsed ONCE for all groups that contain it (soft-reference cache, not only the previous group like `prevParsed`); (2) independent groups run on `min(3, cores/2, heapMB/256)` threads, results are merged in GROUP ORDER with the same de-dup + cap of 200, so the output does not depend on thread timing; (3) a group that throws OutOfMemoryError is NOT dropped: it is run again alone after the others (path bar: `N group(s) re-run for memory`); (4) Stop scan keeps the finished groups.
  - `SsaForm.buildCached(MethodIr)` (soft reference in `MethodIr.ssaRef`): the SSA form of a method is built once, not on every worklist re-run (the engine analysed each method about 1.6 times) and not again for every group. `TaintIr.Run.fingerprintOf` caches the summary fingerprint (was computed twice per analysis).
  - Measured on 1 core (sandbox): -11% with 1 thread, -45% with 2 threads on the synthetic app. On a phone with 6-8 cores the gain from threads is larger; real numbers wait for the path bar (`time: plan Ns + groups Ns on K thread(s)`).
  - Memory: up to K groups are alive at once (K = threads). The IR is read-only after parsing (checked: no code writes to ClassIr / MethodIr / SsaForm after build), so sharing it between threads is safe. If the phone runs out of memory the group is retried alone.
  - NOT done on purpose (would change results): smaller groups, dropping sink-only classes, sharing summaries between groups. Still a known limit: a flow whose chain leaves its group is not seen.
  - Kotlin part is not compiled: if the build fails, look first at the `object : dev.apkrelab.ir.SsaGroups.Host` block in `MainActivity.ssaFindings`.
  - Owner: rebuild, Deep scan on JustMarkets, send the path bar (groups, time, threads) and tell how long the SSA step took.

## Version 0.59.3 (previous)
- 0.59.3 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; 100/100 IR tests pass locally, run once). Version 0.59.3 / code 134. **Owner decision: accuracy before time ("optimization WITH accuracy").** The lossy filters of 0.59.1 / 0.59.2 are OFF for the app: `ScopePlanner.groups(src, entries, max, intentOnly, reachableOnly, nearOnly)` (6 args; the 3-arg overload = all false) and `MainActivity.ssaFindings` calls it with `false, false, false`: SharedPreferences stay sources, classes no entry reaches stay, sink-only classes far from a source stay. Reason a sink-only class matters: a group built around a sink has the whole entry slice, so a source 5 hops away on a path from an entry is inside it; dropping sink batches would lose such long flows. The 4-minute budget is gone too.
  - Lossless speed-up: classes parsed for the previous group are reused by the next one (`prevParsed`, checked: nothing in CallGraph / SsaForm / TaintIr mutates ClassIr / MethodIr), so shared classes (entry slice, bases) are not read and parsed again. Peak memory: at most about two groups while the new group is assembled.
  - Stop scan during the SSA step now KEEPS the flows found so far (path bar: `stopped by you after N of M groups`), before it dropped all of them.
  - Kept from 0.59.2: the path bar ends with `time: plan Ns + groups Ns` (where the time goes).
  - NOT done (accuracy-neutral ideas, wait for the timing numbers): two groups in parallel (doubles memory), restrict analysis inside a group to the methods that matter, a disk cache. Known limit unchanged: a flow whose chain leaves its group is not seen (groups share no summaries).
  - Owner: rebuild, Deep scan on JustMarkets, send the path bar (groups, time) and tell if it finished.

## Version 0.59.2 (previous)
- 0.59.2 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; 99/99 IR tests pass locally, run once). Version 0.59.2 / code 133. Owner asked how to cut the time of the big-app SSA (0.59.1 result not received yet). Done: (1) `CallSkeleton.build(src, entries, includePrefs)`: for big apps SharedPreferences getters are NOT sources of the grouping (weakest source, its findings end up LOW); Intent getters and Provider classes stay. (2) `ScopePlanner.groups(src, entries, max, intentOnly, reachableOnly)`: marked classes that no entry reaches (class-level `forward(entries)`) are left out (`Groups.unreachable`); ignored when no entry is known. The 3-arg `groups` keeps prefs and applies reachableOnly. (3) The path bar now ends with `time: plan Ns + groups Ns`, and says `left out: N not reached by an entry, M sink-only`. New test `intentOnlyDropsPreferencesSourcesAndReachableOnlyDropsClassesNoEntryReaches`.
  - Limits: inside a group TaintIr still treats SharedPreferences as a source; a flow that starts from prefs only is not looked for in a big app. A class reached only by XML / reflection (custom views, navigation fragments) may be wrongly "not reached". NOT done (offered, waiting for the timing numbers): two groups in parallel (doubles memory), a cache of parsed classes shared between groups.
  - Owner: rebuild, run Deep scan on the JustMarkets app, send the path bar (groups, left out, `time: plan .. + groups ..`).

## Version 0.59.1 (previous)
- 0.59.1 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; 98/98 IR tests pass locally, run once). Version 0.59.1 / code 132. **0.59.0 result (owner, device, JustMarkets 60.4 MB, 2474 files)**: the SSA step showed `group 31/467` after 15 minutes (92/467 later): far too slow. Cause (found by reading, not measured): sink APIs (File, URL ...) occur in most classes, so almost every class was "marked", the batches shrank until each group's closure fitted 3000 classes, and every group re-parsed its classes.
  - `ScopePlanner.groups`: a sink-only class counts only when a SOURCE class is within 2 hops (either direction); the others are counted in `Groups.skippedSinks`. Order: source+sink classes, source classes, near sink classes. New test `aSinkOnlyClassFarFromAnySourceIsLeftOutAndANearOneIsKept`.
  - `ssaFindings`: time budget of 4 minutes for the group loop (always runs the first group); on timeout the note says `stopped after N of M groups (time limit)` and the flows found so far are kept.
  - Limit added: a flow whose sink class is more than 2 hops from every source class is no longer looked for in a big app (fields can link far classes). Group count on the real app is still unknown: owner sends the path bar again.

## Version 0.59.0 (previous)
- 0.59.0 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; Java part compiled with javac 21 via `java -m jdk.compiler/com.sun.tools.javac.Main`, 97/97 IR tests pass locally, run once: 91 old + 6 new `IrGroupsTest`). Version 0.59.0 / code 131. **Roadmap P1 step 4, part 2 of 3: the whole big app is covered, no total cap; incremental, group by group.**
  - **Deviation from the plan told to the owner** (be honest about it): the plan said "bottom-up method summaries, then forget the IR". `TaintIr` ALREADY solves per-method summaries with a worklist, and its SSA is built per method and dropped; what costs memory is the parsed `ClassIr` + `CallGraph` of the selected classes, and a graph that spans groups would need `CallGraph` / `TaintIr` to resolve callees that are not parsed (a rewrite, not done). So 0.59.0 bounds the memory by GROUPS instead: each group is parsed, analysed and freed. Summaries are NOT shared between groups.
  - `ScopePlanner.groups(Source, entryTypes, maxPerGroup)` (new, same skeleton as 0.58.0): marked classes (source and/or sink) ordered best first (source AND sink in one class, then the rest by index = package locality), cut into batches; the batch size is found by halving until the group closure fits `maxPerGroup`. Closure of a batch: the batch, callees 1 hop, entry slice of the batch (`backward(batch) AND forward(entries)`), callers 1-2 hops, supertypes, callees 2 hops (tiers in that order, cut at the limit). Every marked class is in exactly one batch, so every class with a source or sink is analysed. `Groups` has `groups`, `total`, `marked`, `entries`, `cappedGroups`, `cancelled`. `plan()` (0.57.0 / 0.58.0) is unchanged and still used by tests.
  - `MainActivity.ssaFindings`: up to 6000 non-library classes = one run as before. Above it: `groups(..., 3000)`, then per group parse -> `SsaBridge.newFlows` -> free; items are de-duplicated across groups by class + method + offset + title (a shared helper can be in several groups), at most 200 kept. One group that throws is counted under `taint-ssa` and the others still run. The path bar shows e.g. `[SSA taint: +3 flow(s) (14 groups of up to 3000 classes, 410 classes with a source or sink of 42000)]` and the progress line shows `Data-flow analysis (SSA) group i/n`. New optional parameter `progress` of `ssaFindings`.
  - Tests `IrGroupsTest` (6): every marked class in some group and no group above the limit, fillers stay out; 10 independent flows with limit 9: one capped `plan()` loses some, the groups find all 10 with `reach YES`; best classes first; a huge neighbourhood caps the group but keeps the marked class (`cappedGroups`); no marked class = no group; cancel.
  - **Known limits**: (1) not measured on a real big app: time (the planner reads every class once, then every group re-parses its classes, so shared infrastructure classes are parsed again per group) and memory are unknown. (2) A flow whose chain leaves its group (more than 2 hops from the batch) is not seen; groups do not share summaries. (3) The findings are NOT shown while the scan runs: they are added when the SSA step ends (the Deep scan result list is built once at the end; streaming would need a UI change in `runDeepScan`, not done). (4) `Taint on SSA` export still parses every non-library class (can run out of memory on a huge app). (5) Kotlin part is not compiled: check the `ssaFindings` call with the trailing lambda and the `Pair` loop first if the build fails.
  - Next (0.60.0): disk cache of per-APK results so a re-scan skips groups whose classes did not change, and/or the streaming UI. Owner: rebuild, run Deep scan on a BIG app (more than 6000 classes) and send the path bar text, the time, and any new taint cards.

## Version 0.58.0 (previous)
- 0.58.0 (unverified on device, NOT compiled with Gradle/Kotlin; Java part compiled with javac 21 via `java -m jdk.compiler/com.sun.tools.javac.Main`, 91/91 IR tests pass locally: 83 old + 8 new `IrSkeletonTest`; JUnit stub, no junit jar in the sandbox). Version 0.58.0 / code 130. **Roadmap P1 step 4, part 1 of 3: light call skeleton, full entry slice.** Plan agreed with the owner for big apps: 0.58.0 skeleton + on-demand read, then 0.59.0 bottom-up method summaries + incremental scan, then 0.60.0 disk cache of the summaries.
  - **Why**: 0.57.0 selected classes by a fixed depth (callees 1, callers 2). A flow whose entry is 3+ hops away lost its path (shown as reach NO + the reduced-scope note) and a helper chain longer than 1 was cut. Shrinking the scope hid flows; the goal is to cover the whole app.
  - New `ir/CallSkeleton.java` (pure Java): ONE pass over the text of every non-library class (text dropped after each class), keeps only int arrays: flags (source / sink), `callees` / `callers` / `parents` per class. Edges: invoke owners, `const-class` (explicit ICC), an invoke on type X also links X's app ancestors and app descendants (class-level CHA, skipped above `MAX_DESCENDANTS` = 64), and every class links its direct supertypes. Over-approximates on purpose: it only decides what gets parsed. `forward()` / `backward()` BFS, `stats()`.
  - `ScopePlanner.plan` now runs on the skeleton (same API, `Plan` has a new field `slice`). New tiers after 0.57.0's A-F: **G entry slice** = every class on a path from an entry to a marked class at ANY depth (`forward(entries)` AND `backward(marked)`), **S** supertypes of everything chosen (CallGraph walks the super chain for inherited methods / interface defaults), **H** callees of D (helper that calls a helper). Cap logic unchanged (6000, tiers in order, `capped` reported). The 0.57.0 tests still pass unchanged (depth-2 callers stay selected even with no entries).
  - `MainActivity.ssaFindings`: only the note changed, e.g. `reduced scope 410/9200 classes, entry slice 85`. Version bump.
  - Tests `IrSkeletonTest` (8): 5-hop entry chain kept whole + a dead 4-hop caller chain still cut at depth 2; end-to-end flow behind a 5-hop path is now `reach YES` (needs the whole path in scope); interface call reaches the implementation; call on a subclass reaches the inherited method in the base; `const-class` edge; huge base class not expanded; counts / stats; cancel returns null.
  - **Known limits (kept honest)**: nothing measured on a real big app (memory / time on the phone unknown). The slice can be large when many components share one app base class. Class-level edges ignore reflection, lambdas via invokedynamic, string-built class names. The parsed set is still held in memory at once (CallGraph needs every selected ClassIr), so the 6000 cap stays until 0.59.0; the planner pass itself already reads text on demand (`agentIndexedText`) and keeps nothing but ints. Method summaries (0.59.0) are what finally lets the cap go.
  - Next: owner rebuilds (6 lab apps are below 6000 classes, so no change expected there) and runs Deep scan on a BIG app; send the path bar (`reduced scope N/M classes, entry slice K`), the time, and any new taint cards or reach changes.

Version 0.57.0 / code 129. **Roadmap P1 step 3: the SSA taint now runs on BIG apps (reduced scope) instead of skipping them.** **0.56.0 results (owner, device)**: build OK. Deep scan on 6 apps, path bar + Taint-on-SSA exports: InjuredAndroid `+1 flow` (RCEActivity CRITICAL, reach YES; report both 4 / only old 0 / only new 0); InsecureShop `+1 flow` (PrivateActivity, reach NO; both 4 / 0 / 0); AndroGoat `no extra flow` (first export was taken before the Deep scan finished, so it showed only new 3; re-export after it finished: both 1, MERGED 2 = the `(x3)` SQL card); InsecureBankv2 `no extra flow` (both 2, MERGED 1); Damn Vulnerable Bank `no extra flow` (both 1, 6383 methods, 1243 ms); OVAA `no extra flow` but the export shows ONLY NEW 1 (`TheftOverwriteProvider.openFile`, file path) - see the open item below. No failures, max 1.2 s.
  - **Open item (owner chose not to fix now)**: the export builds its old list from `secFindings` (after `aggregate`) and drops entries whose `methodKeyAt` fails, while `ssaFindings` uses `found` (before `aggregate`). On OVAA the scan therefore saw an old card for `TheftOverwriteProvider` (no duplicate card was added) and the export did not. Cosmetic: the export may print a false ONLY NEW. Unverified guess: the old card was merged / its method key could not be resolved.
